// apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-thread.tsx
/**
 * Main conversation surface for the assistant workspace.
 *
 * Purpose:
 * - Keep the thread as the primary product surface instead of another card.
 * - Provide the lean topbar, continuous message stream, and thread states.
 * - Dock the composer beneath the thread while preserving message continuity.
 */

"use client";

import * as React from "react";
import {
  BotMessageSquare,
  MessageSquareText,
  PanelLeft,
  RefreshCw,
} from "lucide-react";

import {
  AssistantMessage,
  AssistantPendingTurn,
} from "@/components/assistant/assistant-message";
import { Button } from "@/components/ui/button";
import type {
  AssistantConversationItem,
  AssistantMessageItem,
} from "@/lib/api/assistant";

type AsyncStatus = "idle" | "loading" | "success" | "error";

function AssistantThreadLoadingState(): React.JSX.Element {
  return (
    <div className="space-y-6">
      {Array.from({ length: 4 }).map((_, index) => (
        <div
          key={`assistant-thread-skeleton-${index + 1}`}
          className={`flex ${index % 2 === 0 ? "justify-start" : "justify-end"}`}
        >
          <div className={`space-y-2 ${index % 2 === 0 ? "w-full max-w-3xl" : "w-full max-w-2xl"}`}>
            {index % 2 === 0 ? (
              <>
                <div className="h-3 w-24 rounded-full bg-muted/30" />
                <div className="rounded-2xl bg-muted/22 px-4 py-4">
                  <div className="grid gap-2">
                    <div className="h-4 w-32 rounded-full bg-muted/35" />
                    <div className="h-4 w-full max-w-2xl rounded-full bg-muted/30" />
                    <div className="h-4 w-48 rounded-full bg-muted/25" />
                  </div>
                </div>
              </>
            ) : (
              <>
                <div className="rounded-2xl bg-muted/28 px-4 py-4">
                  <div className="grid gap-2">
                    <div className="h-4 w-56 rounded-full bg-muted/35" />
                    <div className="h-4 w-36 rounded-full bg-muted/28" />
                  </div>
                </div>
                <div className="ml-auto h-3 w-28 rounded-full bg-muted/25" />
              </>
            )}
          </div>
        </div>
      ))}
    </div>
  );
}

export function AssistantThread({
  activeConversation,
  hasConversations,
  isDraftConversation,
  messages,
  messagesStatus,
  messagesError,
  messagesRefreshing,
  pendingQuery,
  onRetry,
  onOpenMobileSidebar,
  composer,
}: {
  activeConversation: AssistantConversationItem | null;
  hasConversations: boolean;
  isDraftConversation: boolean;
  messages: AssistantMessageItem[];
  messagesStatus: AsyncStatus;
  messagesError: string | null;
  messagesRefreshing: boolean;
  pendingQuery: string | null;
  onRetry: () => void;
  onOpenMobileSidebar: () => void;
  composer: React.ReactNode;
}): React.JSX.Element {
  const threadBottomRef = React.useRef<HTMLDivElement | null>(null);
  const previousConversationIdRef = React.useRef<string | null>(null);

  React.useEffect(() => {
    const nextConversationId = activeConversation?.id ?? null;
    const behavior =
      previousConversationIdRef.current !== nextConversationId
        ? "auto"
        : "smooth";

    previousConversationIdRef.current = nextConversationId;
    threadBottomRef.current?.scrollIntoView({
      behavior,
      block: "end",
    });
  }, [activeConversation?.id, messages.length, pendingQuery]);

  return (
    <div className="flex h-full min-h-0 flex-1 flex-col">
      <div className="shrink-0 border-b border-border/50 px-4 py-4 sm:px-6">
        <div className="flex items-start gap-3">
          <Button
            type="button"
            variant="ghost"
            size="icon"
            className="mt-0.5 h-10 w-10 rounded-2xl md:hidden"
            onClick={onOpenMobileSidebar}
            aria-label="Open conversation history"
          >
            <PanelLeft className="h-4.5 w-4.5" />
          </Button>

          <div className="min-w-0 flex-1">
            <p className="truncate text-base font-semibold text-foreground sm:text-lg">
              {activeConversation?.title ?? "New chat"}
            </p>
            <div className="mt-1 flex flex-wrap items-center gap-x-3 gap-y-1 text-xs text-muted-foreground">
              <span>
                {isDraftConversation
                  ? hasConversations
                    ? "Start another conversation with general or official-document questions."
                    : "Start your first conversation with a general or official-document question."
                  : ""}
              </span>
              {messagesRefreshing ? (
                <span className="inline-flex items-center gap-1.5">
                  <RefreshCw className="h-3 w-3 animate-spin" />
                  Refreshing thread
                </span>
              ) : null}
            </div>
          </div>
        </div>
      </div>

      <div className="min-h-0 flex-1 overflow-y-auto px-4 py-5 sm:px-6 sm:py-6">
        <div className="w-full">
          {messagesStatus === "loading" ? (
            <AssistantThreadLoadingState />
          ) : null}

          {messagesStatus === "error" ? (
            <div className="rounded-3xl border border-destructive/20 bg-destructive/6 px-5 py-6">
              <p className="text-sm font-semibold text-destructive">
                Unable to load this conversation.
              </p>
              <p className="mt-2 text-sm leading-6 text-muted-foreground">
                {messagesError}
              </p>
              <Button
                type="button"
                variant="outline"
                className="mt-4 gap-2 rounded-2xl"
                onClick={onRetry}
              >
                <RefreshCw className="h-4 w-4" />
                Retry
              </Button>
            </div>
          ) : null}

          {messagesStatus !== "loading" &&
          messagesStatus !== "error" &&
          messages.length === 0 &&
          !pendingQuery ? (
            <div className="flex min-h-full items-center justify-center py-10">
              <div className="w-full max-w-xl text-center">
                <div className="mx-auto flex h-16 w-16 items-center justify-center rounded-3xl border border-primary/20 bg-primary/6 text-primary">
                  {hasConversations && isDraftConversation ? (
                    <MessageSquareText className="h-7 w-7" />
                  ) : (
                    <BotMessageSquare className="h-7 w-7" />
                  )}
                </div>
                <p className="mt-5 text-xl font-semibold text-foreground">
                  {hasConversations && isDraftConversation
                    ? "Start another conversation"
                    : "Ask your first question"}
                </p>
                <p className="mt-3 text-sm leading-7 text-muted-foreground">
                  Ask a general question or ask about official university
                  documents. Document sources are shown only when matching
                  official material is found.
                </p>
              </div>
            </div>
          ) : null}

          {messagesStatus !== "loading" && messagesStatus !== "error" ? (
            <div className="space-y-6">
              {messages.map((message) => (
                <AssistantMessage key={message.id} message={message} />
              ))}
              {pendingQuery ? (
                <AssistantPendingTurn query={pendingQuery} />
              ) : null}
              <div ref={threadBottomRef} />
            </div>
          ) : null}
        </div>
      </div>

      {composer}
    </div>
  );
}

FILE: apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-message.tsx

// apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-message.tsx
/**
 * Message rendering for the assistant thread.
 *
 * Purpose:
 * - Replace the old badge-heavy card treatment with cleaner chat hierarchy.
 * - Keep assistant citations visually attached to the assistant answer.
 * - Preserve distinct user and assistant visual rhythm without turning every
 *   turn into a dashboard widget.
 */

"use client";

import * as React from "react";
import { BotMessageSquare, LoaderCircle } from "lucide-react";

import { AssistantCitations } from "@/components/assistant/assistant-citations";
import type { AssistantMessageItem } from "@/lib/api/assistant";

function formatTimestamp(value: string): string {
  const parsed = new Date(value);

  if (Number.isNaN(parsed.getTime())) {
    return value;
  }

  return new Intl.DateTimeFormat("en-PK", {
    dateStyle: "medium",
    timeStyle: "short",
  }).format(parsed);
}

export function AssistantMessage({
  message,
}: {
  message: AssistantMessageItem;
}): React.JSX.Element {
  const isUser = message.role === "user";
  const shouldShowFallbackState = !isUser && Boolean(message.fallback_used);
  const shouldShowCitations =
    !isUser && !shouldShowFallbackState && message.citations.length > 0;

  if (isUser) {
    return (
      <div className="flex justify-end">
        <div className="max-w-2xl space-y-2">
          <div className="rounded-3xl bg-primary px-4 py-3 text-primary-foreground shadow-sm sm:px-5 sm:py-4">
            <p className="whitespace-pre-wrap text-sm leading-7 sm:text-[0.95rem]">
              {message.content}
            </p>
          </div>
          <p className="px-1 text-right text-xs text-muted-foreground">
            {formatTimestamp(message.created_at)}
          </p>
        </div>
      </div>
    );
  }

  return (
    <div className="flex justify-start">
      <div className="max-w-4xl space-y-2.5">
        <div className="rounded-3xl border border-border/50 bg-muted/28 px-4 py-3 shadow-sm sm:px-5 sm:py-4">
          <div className="mb-2 flex items-center gap-2 text-xs font-semibold uppercase tracking-[0.18em] text-muted-foreground">
            <BotMessageSquare className="h-3.5 w-3.5 text-primary" />
            <span>Assistant</span>
          </div>
          <p className="whitespace-pre-wrap text-sm leading-7 text-foreground sm:text-[0.95rem]">
            {message.content}
          </p>
          {shouldShowFallbackState ? (
            <div className="mt-4 rounded-2xl border border-amber-500/25 bg-amber-500/8 px-3 py-3 text-sm leading-6 text-amber-950 dark:text-amber-100">
              This answer used the assistant fallback because it could not
              ground the response in the retrieved official university documents
              for this turn.
            </div>
          ) : null}
        </div>

        {shouldShowCitations ? (
          <AssistantCitations citations={message.citations} />
        ) : null}

        <p className="px-1 text-xs text-muted-foreground">
          {formatTimestamp(message.created_at)}
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

export function AssistantPendingTurn({
  query,
}: {
  query: string;
}): React.JSX.Element {
  return (
    <div className="space-y-6">
      <div className="flex justify-end">
        <div className="max-w-2xl rounded-3xl bg-primary px-4 py-3 text-primary-foreground shadow-sm sm:px-5 sm:py-4">
          <p className="whitespace-pre-wrap text-sm leading-7 sm:text-[0.95rem]">
            {query}
          </p>
        </div>
      </div>

      <div className="flex justify-start">
        <div className="max-w-4xl rounded-3xl border border-border/50 bg-muted/28 px-4 py-3 shadow-sm sm:px-5 sm:py-4">
          <div className="mb-3 flex items-center gap-2 text-xs font-semibold uppercase tracking-[0.18em] text-muted-foreground">
            <BotMessageSquare className="h-3.5 w-3.5 text-primary" />
            <span>Assistant</span>
          </div>
          <div className="flex items-center gap-3 text-sm text-muted-foreground">
            <LoaderCircle className="h-4 w-4 animate-spin" />
            <span>Searching documents when needed...</span>
          </div>
        </div>
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

FILE: apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-citations.tsx

// apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-citations.tsx
/**
 * Compact citation attachments for assistant messages.
 *
 * Purpose:
 * - Keep citations visible without breaking the conversation rhythm.
 * - Present source metadata as attached source chips under assistant answers
 *   instead of as a second dashboard panel.
 * - Keep long source lists compact with a local expand/collapse treatment.
 */

"use client";

import * as React from "react";
import { ChevronDown, ChevronUp, FileText } from "lucide-react";

import { Button } from "@/components/ui/button";
import type { AssistantCitationItem } from "@/lib/api/assistant";

const DEFAULT_VISIBLE_CITATIONS = 3;

function buildCitationMeta(citation: AssistantCitationItem): string {
  const parts: string[] = [];

  if (citation.page_number) {
    parts.push(`Page ${citation.page_number}`);
  }

  if (citation.section_label) {
    parts.push(citation.section_label);
  }

  parts.push(`Chunk ${citation.chunk_index}`);

  return parts.join(" | ");
}

export function AssistantCitations({
  citations,
}: {
  citations: AssistantCitationItem[];
}): React.JSX.Element | null {
  const [expanded, setExpanded] = React.useState(false);

  if (citations.length === 0) {
    return null;
  }

  const visibleCitations = expanded
    ? citations
    : citations.slice(0, DEFAULT_VISIBLE_CITATIONS);
  const hiddenCount = citations.length - visibleCitations.length;

  return (
    <div className="mt-3 flex max-w-4xl flex-col gap-2.5">
      <div className="flex flex-wrap gap-2">
        {visibleCitations.map((citation) => (
          <div
            key={`${citation.rank}-${citation.chunk_id ?? "no-chunk"}`}
            className="min-w-0 max-w-full rounded-2xl border border-border/60 bg-background/85 px-3 py-2 shadow-sm"
          >
            <div className="flex items-start gap-2">
              <div className="mt-0.5 flex h-7 w-7 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-xl bg-primary/8 text-primary">
                <FileText className="h-3.5 w-3.5" />
              </div>
              <div className="min-w-0">
                <p className="truncate text-sm font-semibold text-foreground">
                  {citation.document_title}
                </p>
                <p className="mt-0.5 text-xs leading-5 text-muted-foreground">
                  {buildCitationMeta(citation)}
                </p>
                <p className="mt-0.5 truncate text-[11px] leading-5 text-muted-foreground/90">
                  {citation.original_filename}
                </p>
              </div>
            </div>
          </div>
        ))}
      </div>

      {citations.length > DEFAULT_VISIBLE_CITATIONS ? (
        <div>
          <Button
            type="button"
            variant="ghost"
            size="sm"
            className="h-8 rounded-full px-3 text-xs font-semibold text-muted-foreground hover:text-foreground"
            onClick={() => {
              setExpanded((current) => !current);
            }}
          >
            {expanded ? (
              <>
                <ChevronUp className="mr-1.5 h-3.5 w-3.5" />
                Show fewer sources
              </>
            ) : (
              <>
                <ChevronDown className="mr-1.5 h-3.5 w-3.5" />
                Show {hiddenCount} more source{hiddenCount === 1 ? "" : "s"}
              </>
            )}
          </Button>
        </div>
      ) : null}
    </div>
  );
}



============================================================
FILE: apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-composer.tsx
============================================================

// apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-composer.tsx
/**
 * Docked chat composer for the assistant workspace.
 *
 * Purpose:
 * - Replace the detached dashboard-style form with a chat-native composer dock.
 * - Keep the request surface limited to one query while preserving current
 *   submit and pending behavior.
 * - Attach the composer visually to the thread area instead of rendering it as
 *   a separate page widget.
 */

"use client";

import * as React from "react";
import { LoaderCircle, SendHorizontal } from "lucide-react";

import { Button } from "@/components/ui/button";
import { Textarea } from "@/components/ui/textarea";

export function AssistantComposer({
  composerQuery,
  isDraftConversation,
  submitPending,
  canSubmitQuery,
  minQueryLength,
  onComposerQueryChange,
  onSubmit,
}: {
  composerQuery: string;
  isDraftConversation: boolean;
  submitPending: boolean;
  canSubmitQuery: boolean;
  minQueryLength: number;
  onComposerQueryChange: (nextValue: string) => void;
  onSubmit: (event?: React.FormEvent<HTMLFormElement>) => void;
}): React.JSX.Element {
  const trimmedLength = composerQuery.replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim().length;
  const showLengthHint = trimmedLength > 0 && trimmedLength < minQueryLength;

  return (
    <div className="shrink-0 border-t border-border/50 px-3 py-3 sm:px-5 sm:py-4">
      <div className="w-full">
        <form className="grid gap-3" onSubmit={onSubmit}>
          <div className="rounded-3xl border border-border/55 bg-background px-3 py-3 sm:px-4">
            <div className="flex items-center gap-3">
              <Textarea
                className="max-h-64 overflow-y-auto resize-none rounded-none border-0 bg-transparent px-0 py-0 shadow-none focus-visible:ring-0"
                placeholder={
                  isDraftConversation
                    ? "Ask a general question or ask about official university documents..."
                    : "Ask a follow-up question for this conversation..."
                }
                rows={4}
                value={composerQuery}
                onChange={(event) => {
                  onComposerQueryChange(event.target.value);
                }}
                onKeyDown={(event) => {
                  if (event.key === "Enter" && !event.shiftKey) {
                    event.preventDefault();
                    onSubmit();
                  }
                }}
                disabled={submitPending}
              />

              <Button
                type="submit"
                className="h-12 shrink-0 gap-2 rounded-2xl px-4"
                disabled={!canSubmitQuery}
              >
                {submitPending ? (
                  <LoaderCircle className="h-4 w-4 animate-spin" />
                ) : (
                  <SendHorizontal className="h-4 w-4" />
                )}
                <span className="hidden sm:inline">
                  {submitPending ? "Generating..." : "Send"}
                </span>
              </Button>
            </div>
          </div>

          <p className="px-1 text-xs leading-5 text-muted-foreground">
            {showLengthHint
              ? `Enter at least ${minQueryLength} characters to send.`
              : "Press Enter to send. Use Shift+Enter for a new line."}
          </p>
        </form>
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

// apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-sidebar.tsx
/**
 * Conversation history surface for the shared assistant workspace.
 *
 * Purpose:
 * - Provide desktop collapse behavior and mobile-friendly conversation history.
 * - Keep new-chat, refresh, rename, and delete actions in the history surface.
 * - Replace the old card-based history widget with a real chat history panel.
 */

"use client";

import * as React from "react";
import {
  ChevronLeft,
  MessageSquareDashed,
  PanelLeft,
  Plus,
  RefreshCw,
} from "lucide-react";

import { AssistantConversationItemRow } from "@/components/assistant/assistant-conversation-item";
import { Button } from "@/components/ui/button";
import type { AssistantConversationItem } from "@/lib/api/assistant";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

type AsyncStatus = "idle" | "loading" | "success" | "error";

function AssistantSidebarLoadingState({
  collapsed,
}: {
  collapsed: boolean;
}): React.JSX.Element {
  if (collapsed) {
    return (
      <div className="grid gap-2">
        {Array.from({ length: 6 }).map((_, index) => (
          <div
            key={`assistant-sidebar-rail-skeleton-${index + 1}`}
            className="border-l-2 border-transparent px-2 py-1"
          >
            <div className="flex h-10 items-center justify-center">
              <div className="h-6 w-6 rounded-full bg-muted/35" />
            </div>
          </div>
        ))}
      </div>
    );
  }

  return (
    <div className="grid gap-1">
      {Array.from({ length: 5 }).map((_, index) => (
        <div
          key={`assistant-sidebar-skeleton-${index + 1}`}
          className="border-l-2 border-transparent px-3 py-2.5"
        >
          <div className="grid gap-2">
            <div className="flex items-center justify-between gap-3">
              <div className="h-4 w-34 rounded-full bg-muted/35" />
              <div className="h-3 w-16 rounded-full bg-muted/25" />
            </div>
            <div className="h-3.5 w-28 rounded-full bg-muted/28" />
          </div>
        </div>
      ))}
    </div>
  );
}

export function AssistantSidebar({
  conversations,
  conversationsStatus,
  conversationsError,
  conversationsRefreshing,
  activeConversationId,
  submitPending,
  workspaceLabel = "Assistant",
  collapsed = false,
  mobile = false,
  onNewConversation,
  onRefresh,
  onSelectConversation,
  onRenameConversation,
  onDeleteConversation,
  onToggleCollapse,
}: {
  conversations: AssistantConversationItem[];
  conversationsStatus: AsyncStatus;
  conversationsError: string | null;
  conversationsRefreshing: boolean;
  activeConversationId: string | null;
  submitPending: boolean;
  workspaceLabel?: string;
  collapsed?: boolean;
  mobile?: boolean;
  onNewConversation: () => void;
  onRefresh: () => void;
  onSelectConversation: (conversationId: string) => void;
  onRenameConversation: (conversation: AssistantConversationItem) => void;
  onDeleteConversation: (conversation: AssistantConversationItem) => void;
  onToggleCollapse?: () => void;
}): React.JSX.Element {
  if (collapsed && !mobile) {
    return (
      <div className="flex h-full min-h-0 w-full flex-col items-center gap-3 py-3">
        <div className="grid gap-2">
          <Button
            type="button"
            variant="outline"
            size="icon"
            className="h-11 w-11 rounded-2xl"
            onClick={onToggleCollapse}
            aria-label="Expand conversation history"
            title="Expand conversation history"
          >
            <PanelLeft className="h-4 w-4" />
          </Button>
          <Button
            type="button"
            size="icon"
            className="h-11 w-11 rounded-2xl"
            onClick={onNewConversation}
            disabled={submitPending}
            aria-label="Start a new conversation"
            title="Start a new conversation"
          >
            <Plus className="h-4 w-4" />
          </Button>
          <Button
            type="button"
            variant="outline"
            size="icon"
            className="h-11 w-11 rounded-2xl"
            onClick={onRefresh}
            disabled={conversationsRefreshing}
            aria-label="Refresh conversations"
            title="Refresh conversations"
          >
            <RefreshCw
              className={cn(
                "h-4 w-4",
                conversationsRefreshing && "animate-spin",
              )}
            />
          </Button>
        </div>

        <div className="min-h-0 w-full flex-1 overflow-y-auto pt-1">
          {conversationsStatus === "loading" ? (
            <AssistantSidebarLoadingState collapsed />
          ) : null}

          {conversationsStatus === "error" ? (
            <div className="grid gap-2">
              <div className="rounded-2xl border border-destructive/30 bg-destructive/8 px-2 py-3 text-center text-xs leading-5 text-destructive">
                {conversationsError || "Unable to load history."}
              </div>
              <Button
                type="button"
                variant="outline"
                size="icon"
                className="h-11 w-full rounded-2xl"
                onClick={onRefresh}
                aria-label="Retry loading conversations"
                title="Retry loading conversations"
              >
                <RefreshCw className="h-4 w-4" />
              </Button>
            </div>
          ) : null}

          {conversationsStatus === "success" ? (
            <div className="grid gap-2">
              {conversations.map((conversation) => (
                <AssistantConversationItemRow
                  key={conversation.id}
                  conversation={conversation}
                  isActive={activeConversationId === conversation.id}
                  collapsed
                  onSelect={onSelectConversation}
                  onRename={onRenameConversation}
                  onDelete={onDeleteConversation}
                />
              ))}
            </div>
          ) : null}
        </div>
      </div>
    );
  }

  return (
    <div className="flex h-full min-h-0 w-full flex-col">
      <div className="shrink-0 border-b border-border/50 px-4 py-4">
        <div className="flex items-start justify-between gap-3">
          <div className="min-w-0">
            <p className="text-[11px] font-semibold uppercase tracking-[0.22em] text-primary">
              {workspaceLabel}
            </p>
            <h2 className="mt-1 text-base font-semibold text-foreground">
              Conversation history
            </h2>
          </div>

          {!mobile && onToggleCollapse ? (
            <Button
              type="button"
              variant="ghost"
              size="icon"
              className="h-10 w-10 rounded-2xl"
              onClick={onToggleCollapse}
              aria-label="Collapse conversation history"
              title="Collapse conversation history"
            >
              <ChevronLeft className="h-4.5 w-4.5" />
            </Button>
          ) : null}
        </div>

        <div className="mt-4 flex items-center gap-2">
          <Button
            type="button"
            className="flex-1 gap-2 rounded-2xl"
            onClick={onNewConversation}
            disabled={submitPending}
          >
            <Plus className="h-4 w-4" />
            New chat
          </Button>
          <Button
            type="button"
            variant="outline"
            size="icon"
            className="h-11 w-11 rounded-2xl"
            onClick={onRefresh}
            disabled={conversationsRefreshing}
            aria-label="Refresh conversations"
            title="Refresh conversations"
          >
            <RefreshCw
              className={cn(
                "h-4 w-4",
                conversationsRefreshing && "animate-spin",
              )}
            />
          </Button>
        </div>
      </div>

      <div className="min-h-0 flex-1 overflow-y-auto ">
        {conversationsStatus === "loading" ? (
          <AssistantSidebarLoadingState collapsed={false} />
        ) : null}

        {conversationsStatus === "error" ? (
          <div className="rounded-3xl border border-destructive/20 bg-destructive/7 px-4 py-5">
            <p className="text-sm font-semibold text-destructive">
              Unable to load conversations.
            </p>
            <p className="mt-2 text-sm leading-6 text-muted-foreground">
              {conversationsError}
            </p>
            <Button
              type="button"
              variant="outline"
              className="mt-4 gap-2 rounded-2xl"
              onClick={onRefresh}
            >
              <RefreshCw className="h-4 w-4" />
              Retry
            </Button>
          </div>
        ) : null}

        {conversationsStatus === "success" && conversations.length === 0 ? (
          <div className="flex min-h-full items-center justify-center px-2 py-8">
            <div className="max-w-xs text-center">
              <div className="mx-auto flex h-14 w-14 items-center justify-center rounded-3xl border border-primary/20 bg-primary/6 text-primary">
                <MessageSquareDashed className="h-6 w-6" />
              </div>
              <p className="mt-4 text-base font-semibold text-foreground">
                No conversations yet
              </p>
              <p className="mt-2 text-sm leading-6 text-muted-foreground">
                Start with one clear question. The assistant will store the
                conversation after the first completed answer.
              </p>
            </div>
          </div>
        ) : null}

        {conversationsStatus === "success" && conversations.length > 0 ? (
          <div className="grid gap-1.5">
            {conversations.map((conversation) => (
              <AssistantConversationItemRow
                key={conversation.id}
                conversation={conversation}
                isActive={activeConversationId === conversation.id}
                onSelect={onSelectConversation}
                onRename={onRenameConversation}
                onDelete={onDeleteConversation}
              />
            ))}
          </div>
        ) : null}
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

// apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-conversation-item.tsx
/**
 * Conversation-history row for the assistant sidebar.
 *
 * Purpose:
 * - Present one conversation in either expanded row mode or collapsed rail
 *   mode.
 * - Keep rename/delete actions inside the history surface.
 * - Improve scannability over the previous dashboard-card list treatment.
 */

"use client";

import * as React from "react";
import { MoreHorizontal, SquarePen, Trash2 } from "lucide-react";

import { Button } from "@/components/ui/button";
import {
  DropdownMenu,
  DropdownMenuContent,
  DropdownMenuItem,
  DropdownMenuTrigger,
} from "@/components/ui/dropdown-menu";
import type { AssistantConversationItem } from "@/lib/api/assistant";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

function formatRelativeTime(value: string): string {
  const parsed = new Date(value);

  if (Number.isNaN(parsed.getTime())) {
    return value;
  }

  const diffMs = parsed.getTime() - Date.now();
  const diffMinutes = Math.round(diffMs / 60000);
  const formatter = new Intl.RelativeTimeFormat("en", {
    numeric: "auto",
  });

  if (Math.abs(diffMinutes) < 60) {
    return formatter.format(diffMinutes, "minute");
  }

  const diffHours = Math.round(diffMinutes / 60);

  if (Math.abs(diffHours) < 24) {
    return formatter.format(diffHours, "hour");
  }

  const diffDays = Math.round(diffHours / 24);
  return formatter.format(diffDays, "day");
}

function getConversationMonogram(title: string): string {
  const normalized = title.trim();

  if (!normalized) {
    return "?";
  }

  return normalized.charAt(0).toUpperCase();
}

export function AssistantConversationItemRow({
  conversation,
  isActive,
  collapsed = false,
  onSelect,
  onRename,
  onDelete,
}: {
  conversation: AssistantConversationItem;
  isActive: boolean;
  collapsed?: boolean;
  onSelect: (conversationId: string) => void;
  onRename: (conversation: AssistantConversationItem) => void;
  onDelete: (conversation: AssistantConversationItem) => void;
}): React.JSX.Element {
  const [mounted, setMounted] = React.useState(false);

  React.useEffect(() => {
    setMounted(true);
  }, []);

  const relativeTime = mounted
    ? formatRelativeTime(conversation.last_message_at)
    : "Recently updated";

  if (collapsed) {
    return (
      <button
        type="button"
        onClick={() => {
          onSelect(conversation.id);
        }}
        className={cn(
          "border-l-3  text-muted-foreground transition-colors",
          isActive
            ? "border-primary text-primary"
            : "border-transparent hover:border-primary/30 hover:text-foreground",
        )}
        aria-label={conversation.title}
        title={conversation.title}
      >
        <span className="flex h-10 w-full items-center justify-center text-sm font-bold">
          {getConversationMonogram(conversation.title)}
        </span>
      </button>
    );
  }

  return (
    <div
      className={cn(
        "group/item border-l-3 transition-colors",
        isActive
          ? "border-primary bg-primary/6"
          : "border-transparent bg-transparent hover:border-primary/30 hover:bg-background/40",
      )}
    >
      <div className="flex items-start gap-2 px-3 py-2.5">
        <button
          type="button"
          onClick={() => {
            onSelect(conversation.id);
          }}
          className="min-w-0 flex-1 text-left"
        >
          <div className="flex items-start justify-between gap-3">
            <p className="truncate text-sm font-semibold text-foreground">
              {conversation.title}
            </p>
            <span
              className="shrink-0 text-[11px] font-medium text-muted-foreground"
              suppressHydrationWarning
            >
              {relativeTime}
            </span>
          </div>
          <p className="mt-1 line-clamp-2 text-sm leading-6 text-muted-foreground">
            {conversation.last_message_preview || "No preview available yet."}
          </p>
        </button>

        <DropdownMenu>
          <DropdownMenuTrigger asChild>
            <Button
              type="button"
              variant="ghost"
              size="icon"
              className="mt-1 h-8 w-8 rounded-xl opacity-100 transition-opacity md:opacity-0 md:group-hover/item:opacity-100 md:group-focus-within/item:opacity-100"
              aria-label="Conversation actions"
            >
              <MoreHorizontal className="h-4 w-4" />
            </Button>
          </DropdownMenuTrigger>
          <DropdownMenuContent align="end" className="w-44 rounded-2xl">
            <DropdownMenuItem
              className="gap-2 rounded-xl"
              onSelect={() => {
                onRename(conversation);
              }}
            >
              <SquarePen className="h-4 w-4" />
              Rename
            </DropdownMenuItem>
            <DropdownMenuItem
              className="gap-2 rounded-xl text-destructive focus:text-destructive"
              onSelect={() => {
                onDelete(conversation);
              }}
            >
              <Trash2 className="h-4 w-4" />
              Delete
            </DropdownMenuItem>
          </DropdownMenuContent>
        </DropdownMenu>
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

# Plan.md

# Auth Migration Plan

## Purpose

This document is the implementation plan for migrating the frontend auth flow of the UAF Smart E-Library & University Information Assistant from a browser-only Supabase client model to a structured Supabase SSR + middleware + normalized auth-state model.

This is not a brainstorming note. It is the working migration contract for the next coding passes.

## Historical Baseline Audit

This section records the pre-migration frontend auth problems that motivated the migration. It is historical context, not the current implemented state.

### What exists now

- Supabase SSR browser client in `apps/web/src/lib/supabase/client.ts`
- Supabase SSR server helper in `apps/web/src/lib/supabase/server.ts`
- Supabase SSR middleware helper in `apps/web/src/lib/supabase/middleware.ts`
- Client auth helpers in `apps/web/src/lib/api/auth.ts`
- Client hooks in `apps/web/src/hooks/useAuth.ts`
- Shared auth boundary files:
  - `apps/web/src/lib/auth/types.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/lib/auth/normalize-auth.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/lib/auth/timezone.ts`
- Client-side normalized auth store:
  - `apps/web/src/components/providers/auth-provider.tsx`
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/use-app-auth.ts`
- Guest-route auth state model:
  - `apps/web/src/lib/auth/route-state.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/use-guest-route-state.ts`
- Auth preference and redirect helpers:
  - `apps/web/src/lib/auth/preferences.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/lib/auth/navigation.ts`
- Hardened password-link state UI:
  - `apps/web/src/components/auth/password-link-state-card.tsx`
- Server auth guard helpers:
  - `apps/web/src/lib/auth/server-guard.ts`
- Shared auth-state screen shell:
  - `apps/web/src/components/auth/auth-state-screen.tsx`
- Session teardown and recovery helpers:
  - `apps/web/src/lib/auth/session-errors.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/lib/auth/session-client.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/use-auth-session-actions.ts`
- Guest auth screens:
  - `/login`
  - `/forgot-password`
  - `/reset-password`
  - `/setup-password`
- Invite/setup flow now correctly uses `verifyOtp(...)` before `updateUser(...)`
- Legal routes exist:
  - `/privacy-policy`
  - `/terms-of-use`
  - `/library-rules`

### What does not exist yet

- No final protected feature modules beyond placeholder dashboard roots
- No full protected layout UX beyond coarse role gating

### Pre-migration problems

1. Session authority is client-only.
   - The app currently uses `supabase.auth.getSession()` in client code.
   - There is no server-visible route protection.

2. Business role is not normalized.
   - Supabase's built-in `user.role` is not the business role.
   - The business role must come from `app_metadata.role`.
   - Role casing is expected to be inconsistent unless normalized at the frontend boundary.

3. Route protection does not exist.
   - There is no `middleware.ts`.
   - There are no protected route groups.
   - There is no coarse auth or role gating at the edge.

4. Setup-password and reset-password logic is isolated but not part of a larger auth system.
   - These flows work as standalone screens, but they do not yet feed a global auth state model.

5. Remember-me behavior is not defined as a true app preference.
   - The current app does not implement a persistence preference model.

6. Auth lifecycle handling is missing.
   - No `onAuthStateChange(...)` listener
   - No `TOKEN_REFRESHED` integration
   - No cross-tab sign-out handling
   - No stale-session recovery model

## Locked Architectural Decisions

These decisions are fixed for implementation.

1. Supabase is the source of truth for session state.
2. Supabase SSR is the only allowed session storage model for protected frontend routing.
3. No custom access-token / refresh-token cookie system will be created.
4. Middleware will do coarse routing only.
5. Layouts and screens will handle exact UX states.
6. Business role will be normalized from `app_metadata.role`.
7. Email verification state will be derived from `email_confirmed_at`, not metadata guesses.
8. Invite/setup-password remains a public verification flow.
9. Backend and PostgreSQL RLS remain the true authorization layer for protected data.
10. App-level cookies may exist only for non-secret preferences such as:
    - `remember_me`
    - `preferred_timezone`
    - optional `last_role`

## Security Constraints

The following rules must be enforced during implementation.

1. Do not trust frontend role alone for protected data access.
2. Do not trust custom cookies as authorization truth.
3. Do not duplicate Supabase session secrets into homemade cookies.
4. Do not build a manual refresh-token engine.
5. Do not use `user.role` as the app role.
6. Do not use `user_metadata.email_verified` as the verification gate.
7. Do not allow protected role routes to render based only on stale client memory.
8. Do not break the invite/setup flow by gating `/setup-password` behind ordinary auth redirects.

## Auth State Separation Rules

The following auth conditions must be treated as different states:

1. `unauthenticated`
   - no valid session exists

2. `verification_required`
   - user exists but email is not confirmed
   - route to `/auth/verify-required`

3. `password_setup_required`
   - invited user must complete first-time password setup
   - route through `/setup-password` invite flow or an explicit helper screen
   - do not confuse this with email verification

4. `authenticated`
   - valid session exists, email is confirmed, role is normalized

5. `access_denied`
   - valid session exists but role is missing/invalid for app routing

These states must not be merged into one generic Ã¢â‚¬Å“auth problemÃ¢â‚¬Â bucket.

## Target Route Model

### Public routes

- `/login`
- `/setup-password`
- `/reset-password`
- `/forgot-password`
- `/privacy-policy`
- `/terms-of-use`
- `/library-rules`

### Auth transition routes

- `/auth/verify-required`
- `/auth/access-denied`
- `/auth/session-expired`
- optional `/auth/callback` only if a dedicated callback exchange route becomes necessary

### Protected role roots

- `/student/...`
- `/librarian/...`
- `/admin/...`

## Target Auth State Shape

One normalized frontend auth object will be used across the app.

```ts
type AppAuthStatus =
  | "unknown"
  | "unauthenticated"
  | "verification_required"
  | "password_setup_required"
  | "authenticated"
  | "access_denied"
  | "expired";

type AppRole = "student" | "librarian" | "admin";

interface AppAuthState {
  status: AppAuthStatus;
  userId: string | null;
  email: string | null;
  fullName: string | null;
  role: AppRole | null;
  emailConfirmed: boolean;
  expiresAtUtc: string | null;
  rememberMe: boolean;
  timezone: string;
  sessionId: string | null;
}
```

Raw Supabase payloads will not be spread across screens.

## Role Normalization Rules

Frontend normalization must map role variants into one canonical enum.

Accepted input examples:

- `STUDENT`
- `student`
- `LIBRARIAN`
- `librarian`
- `ADMIN`
- `admin`

Canonical output:

- `student`
- `librarian`
- `admin`

Unknown or missing role must not be silently accepted.
It must route to `/auth/access-denied`.

## Timezone Resolution Rules

Use this order:

1. explicit user-selected timezone if later supported
2. browser timezone from `Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().timeZone`
3. fallback `Asia/Karachi`
4. final fallback `UTC`

All stored timestamps remain UTC.
Only display formatting uses the resolved timezone.

## Remember-Me Rules

Remember-me is a persistence preference, not a custom token policy.

Rules:

1. The app will not create a homemade token-cookie system.
2. Supabase session handling remains the source of truth.
3. `remember_me` may be stored as a non-secret app preference cookie.
4. If `remember_me = true`, the app should restore the user session normally across revisits using the Supabase SSR session model.
5. If `remember_me = false`, the app should not fake security claims like Ã¢â‚¬Å“session will definitely disappear on browser closeÃ¢â‚¬Â unless that behavior is explicitly implemented and verified.
6. The checkbox must have real UX meaning in the implementation, or it should not be shown.

## Implementation Passes

### Pass 1: SSR Auth Foundation

Status:

- Implemented

Implemented:

- Established one session authority foundation using Supabase SSR.

Delivered:

- Installed `@supabase/ssr`
- Added browser and server helpers:
  - `apps/web/src/lib/supabase/client.ts` (refactor)
  - `apps/web/src/lib/supabase/server.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/lib/supabase/middleware.ts`
- Added shared auth types:
  - `apps/web/src/lib/auth/types.ts`
- Added auth normalization mapper:
  - `apps/web/src/lib/auth/normalize-auth.ts`
- Added timezone resolver:
  - `apps/web/src/lib/auth/timezone.ts`
- Updated `apps/web/package.json` and lockfile to include the SSR dependency

Result:

- Browser client still works
- Server code can validate session/user through Supabase SSR
- No custom security cookie layer was introduced
- Lint and production build passed after implementation

### Pass 2: Auth Store and Session Mapping

Status:

- Implemented

Implemented:

- Normalized auth state once and made it reusable across the app shell.

Delivered:

- Added lightweight auth store/provider:

### New files likely to be added

- `apps/web/middleware.ts`
- `apps/web/src/lib/supabase/server.ts`
- `apps/web/src/lib/supabase/middleware.ts`
- `apps/web/src/lib/auth/types.ts`

- `apps/web/src/app/(auth-state)/auth/access-denied/page.tsx`

- `apps/web/src/app/(librarian)/librarian/dashboard/page.tsx`
- `apps/web/src/app/(admin)/admin/dashboard/page.tsx`

## Known Risks to Watch During Coding

1. Invite flow can break if middleware treats `/setup-password` like a protected route.
2. Role normalization can break redirect logic if backend role casing remains inconsistent.
3. Server-side checks can be wrong if `getSession()` is trusted on the server instead of validated user retrieval.
4. Parallel client-side and server-side auth assumptions can create split-brain behavior if SSR migration is partial.
5. A broken auth provider can trap the app in loading loops if the store does not handle `SIGNED_OUT` and `TOKEN_REFRESHED` properly.
6. Protected route groups are useless until real dashboard roots exist.

## Out of Scope for This Migration

These are not part of the auth foundation pass unless specifically requested:

- Full admin dashboard implementation
- Full student/librarian modules
- Deep profile settings
- OAuth / social login
- Cookie consent systems
- Dynamic legal-page contacts or policy CMS

## Definition of Done

This migration is complete only when all of the following are true:

1. Session authority is Supabase SSR, not client-only local logic.
2. Middleware exists and protects role prefixes.
3. Frontend role is normalized from `app_metadata.role`.
4. Verification is derived from `email_confirmed_at`.
5. Invite/setup flow remains functional and public.
6. No duplicate homemade token storage exists.
7. Auth lifecycle events update normalized app state correctly.
8. Protected dashboards exist as valid redirect targets.
9. Access denied and verification required states are explicit screens.
10. Build, lint, and route behavior are all verified after each pass.

# Auth Security Hardening Plan

## Purpose

This document section is the implementation plan for hardening the auth-adjacent security posture of the UAF Smart E-Library & University Information Assistant across both backend and frontend surfaces.

This is not a generic checklist. It is derived from the current codebase state and the security constraints defined in `AGENT.md`.

## Security Audit Snapshot

### Security controls already present

- Backend JWT validation exists in `apps/api/core/middleware.py`
  - issuer and audience are checked
  - JWKS are cached and refreshed
  - request IDs are injected
- Backend request validation exists through Pydantic schemas in route modules
- Frontend auth uses Supabase SSR-backed session authority and does not duplicate access or refresh tokens into homemade cookies
- Frontend public config currently uses only expected public environment variables:
  - `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`
  - `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`
  - `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE_URL`
- Document upload filenames are sanitized and allowed extensions / MIME groups are validated in `apps/api/modules/documents/service.py`
- Role gating exists in frontend middleware and protected layouts

### Security gaps currently visible

1. No backend rate limiting exists yet.
   - There is no shared rate-limit middleware or endpoint-level throttle.
   - This affects public auth-adjacent routes and high-cost AI routes most severely.

2. Public account-status lookup is enumerable and unthrottled.
   - `POST /api/auth/account-status` currently reveals:
     - `has_account`
     - `is_deleted`
   - This is intentional for current UX, but it is still an abuse surface and must be heavily rate-limited and monitored.

3. AI endpoints are authenticated but still unthrottled.
   - `POST /api/ai/retrieval/search`
   - `POST /api/ai/query`
   - These routes interact with embeddings / generation cost paths and need stronger protection against scraping and billing abuse.

4. Auth input normalization is still loose.
   - Auth request schemas validate structure, but fields such as names, department labels, and codes are not yet consistently trimmed and normalized at schema boundaries.
   - Public auth email lookup also needs stricter normalization and abuse-aware handling.

5. Security response headers are not centrally enforced.
   - There is no explicit middleware for headers such as:
     - `X-Frame-Options`
     - `X-Content-Type-Options`
     - `Referrer-Policy`
     - controlled `Permissions-Policy`
   - CSP needs careful treatment because of Next.js and current frontend behavior, so it should be planned explicitly rather than improvised.

6. File upload validation is incomplete at the policy level.
   - Allowed file types exist.
   - Filename sanitization exists.
   - But there is no explicit max file-size ceiling enforced in config + schema + service.

7. Dependency hygiene has not been formally audited.
   - `requirements.txt` and `apps/web/package.json` need a targeted security/deprecation review.
   - Redundant or risky packages should be identified before production hardening is declared complete.

## Locked Security Decisions For This Phase

1. Rate limiting must be implemented on the backend, not faked in the frontend.
2. Public account-status behavior will be treated as an intentional UX tradeoff and protected with throttling, logging, and tighter abuse controls rather than silently ignored.
3. AI routes must receive the strongest rate limits in this phase because they are cost-sensitive.
4. Input validation hardening must preserve the current database-centric architecture.
   - No business logic moves from PostgreSQL into FastAPI.
5. No secrets will be added to frontend code.
6. Upload validation must be enforced server-side before any signed upload URL is issued.
7. Any security middleware added must remain compatible with:
   - Supabase auth flows
   - invite/setup-password redirects
   - current SSR frontend flow

## Security Enforcement Constraints

The following operational constraints are mandatory for this security phase:

1. Production rate limiting must not depend on per-process in-memory state alone.
   - The limiter must use a shared backend store or another deployment-safe mechanism.
   - A multi-instance deployment must not silently weaken throttling.

2. Request identity for throttling must be route-aware.
   - Public routes must key primarily on trusted client IP and normalized request fingerprint.
   - Authenticated routes must key on both user identity and trusted client fingerprint.
   - Sensitive public probes such as `/api/auth/account-status` must not rely on raw email text as the only throttle key.

3. Trusted client IP extraction must be proxy-aware.
   - The implementation must define which proxy headers are trusted.
   - The implementation must not blindly trust spoofable forwarded headers from arbitrary clients.

4. Rate-limit failures must be observable.
   - Violations must be structured-logged with route, limiter tier, client fingerprint, and outcome.
   - Logs must avoid leaking raw secrets or sensitive payload contents.

5. Security hardening must remain compatible with current deployment realities.
   - Supabase auth flows must keep working.
   - SSR frontend requests must keep working.
   - Invite/setup-password and reset-password flows must not be broken by over-strict request classification.

## Security Implementation Passes

### Pass S1: Security Baseline and Shared Backend Controls

Status:

- Implemented

Implemented:

- Add shared backend security primitives so auth, AI, and upload routes can be protected consistently.

Delivered:

- Added shared rate-limit foundation in:
  - `apps/api/core/rate_limit.py`
- Added proxy-aware request fingerprint utilities for future throttling and abuse logging:
  - trusted client IP extraction
  - stable request fingerprint generation
  - typed route sensitivity policy resolution
- Added backend security-header middleware with safe defaults in:
  - `apps/api/core/security.py`
- Registered the security-header middleware in:
  - `apps/api/main.py`
- Added typed config for shared security controls in:
  - `apps/api/core/config.py`
  - `apps/api/.env.example`
  - `.env.example`
- Added environment-driven security settings for:
  - rate-limit windows and request ceilings by tier
  - trusted proxy header allowlist
  - HSTS behavior
  - upload max-size limit
- Added a reusable standardized rate-limit response envelope in:
  - `apps/api/core/rate_limit.py`

Result:

- Security headers are added centrally without breaking auth flows
- Shared security settings are typed and environment-driven
- Backend has one reusable foundation for later endpoint throttles
- Standardized 429 responses exist for later enforcement passes

### Pass S1A: Origin Trust and CORS Hardening

Status:

- Implemented

Implemented:

- Ensure browser-origin access to backend routes is explicitly controlled and does not undermine auth or rate-limiting protections.

Delivered:

- Audited current backend CORS behavior against actual frontend request usage.
- Replaced permissive wildcard methods/headers with explicit environment-driven allowlists in:
  - `apps/api/core/config.py`
  - `apps/api/main.py`
- Added typed config for:
  - `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`
  - `CORS_ALLOWED_METHODS`
  - `CORS_ALLOWED_HEADERS`
- Added fail-fast production validation so:
  - wildcard origins are rejected
  - production CORS origins must be explicitly set
  - production origins must use HTTPS
  - localhost origins are rejected in production
- Preserved compatibility with current frontend request needs:
  - `Authorization`
  - `Content-Type`
  - `Accept`
  - optional `X-Request-ID`
- Documented trusted origin examples for:
  - local development
  - preview / staging
  - production
    in:
  - `apps/api/.env.example`
  - `.env.example`

Result:

- Backend origins are explicitly controlled
- No wildcard production CORS policy remains unless there is a documented reason
- Auth flows and SSR requests continue to work under the tightened origin policy
- Production startup now fails fast on unsafe origin policy

### Pass S2: Auth Abuse Protection and Input Hardening

Status:

- Implemented

Goal:

- Protect auth-adjacent routes from brute-force, enumeration, and malformed-input abuse.

Work:

- Applied PostgreSQL-backed rate-limit enforcement to:
  - `POST /api/auth/account-status`
  - admin user-management auth routes under `/api/admin/users/*`
- Added a typed rate-limit exception path in:
  - `apps/api/main.py`
- Tightened auth request normalization and validation in:
  - `apps/api/modules/auth/schemas.py`
- Added strict request-model handling with:
  - `extra="forbid"`
  - normalized lowercase emails
  - trimmed and whitespace-collapsed bounded text fields
  - normalized roll numbers
  - normalized uppercase employee codes
- Reduced auth logging leakage by replacing raw email logging with hashed email identifiers in:
  - `apps/api/modules/auth/routes.py`
  - `apps/api/modules/auth/service.py`
- Preserved current client-facing auth messages while adding structured abuse-observable rate-limit violations through:
  - `apps/api/core/rate_limit.py`

Acceptance:

- Public auth-status probing is throttled and logged with hashed subject identifiers
- Admin auth-management routes have sensitivity-appropriate limits
- Auth request payloads are normalized and bounded more strictly

### Pass S2A: Injection and Output-Sink Audit

Status:

- Implemented

Goal:

- Close the gap between basic schema validation and actual SQL injection / XSS hardening obligations.

Work:

- Audited backend query construction across the currently exposed library/auth surfaces, including:
  - `apps/api/modules/auth/service.py`
  - `apps/api/modules/books/service.py`
  - `apps/api/modules/documents/service.py`
  - `apps/api/modules/results/service.py`
  - `apps/api/modules/ai/service.py`
  - route modules that pass user-controlled values into service/database layers
- Verified that reviewed database access paths use:
  - asyncpg positional parameters (`$1`, `$2`, ...)
  - PostgreSQL RPC calls
  - no reviewed raw SQL string interpolation or `execute(f"...")` pattern
- Audited user-controlled logging paths and reduced leakage in:
  - `apps/api/modules/results/routes.py`
  - `apps/api/modules/results/service.py`
  - `apps/api/modules/documents/routes.py`
  - `apps/api/modules/documents/service.py`
    by replacing raw registration numbers and raw filenames with hashed identifiers
- Confirmed the earlier auth hardening already removed raw email logging from auth routes/services
- Audited frontend rendering sinks across auth and legal screens plus shared components and confirmed:
  - no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`
  - no raw `innerHTML` mutation path
  - no HTML parser/sanitizer bypass path currently in use
  - user-controlled values render through ordinary React JSX text escaping
- Recorded the audit outcome in this plan so SQL injection and XSS review are explicit rather than implied

Acceptance:

- No reviewed raw SQL path handling user input remains unreviewed
- SQL parameterization expectations are explicit and verified for the currently exposed routes/services
- User-controlled frontend output sinks are reviewed for XSS risk and no unsafe HTML sink is currently present
- The security-hardening phase explicitly satisfies the AGENT.md SQL injection / XSS review requirement for the audited surfaces

### Pass S3: AI Route Protection and Cost-Control Throttling

Status:

- Implemented

Goal:

- Prevent scraping, bot abuse, and model-cost amplification on AI routes.

Work:

- Applied strong authenticated PostgreSQL-backed rate limits to:
  - `POST /api/ai/retrieval/search`
  - `POST /api/ai/query`
- Wired AI retrieval to the `ai_retrieval` tier and grounded answer generation to the stricter `ai_generation` tier in:

- Extended AI route logs so they now carry:
  - requester identity
  - route
  - limiter tier
  - status outcome
  - latency for successful completions
- Extended shared rate-limit violation logging in:
  - `apps/api/core/rate_limit.py`
    so abuse-sensitive outcomes now include observed request count alongside ceiling and retry window
- Preserved stable JSON `429` envelopes through the shared rate-limit exception handler already added in the earlier pass
- Added explicit `429` response documentation to the AI route metadata

Acceptance:

- AI routes are throttled according to sensitivity
- Cost-sensitive answer generation path is better protected than ordinary auth routes
- Rate-limit violations are observable in logs and return stable error responses

### Pass S4: Upload Surface Hardening and Storage Enforcement

Status:

- Implemented

Goal:

- Tighten server-side and storage-level validation for upload surfaces that intersect with auth-controlled roles.

Work:

- Enforced the explicit configured max file-size policy at the request-schema boundary in:
  - `apps/api/modules/documents/schemas.py`
- Tightened upload request validation with:
  - `extra="forbid"`
  - normalized required file metadata
  - normalized optional metadata fields (`title`, `document_type`, `audience_scope`, `department`)
- Preserved request-time validation in:
  - `apps/api/modules/documents/service.py`
    and extended it so oversized files are rejected deterministically with a dedicated upload-size error path
- Added storage metadata retrieval in:
  - `apps/api/services/storage_service.py`
    so finalize/index no longer trusts object existence alone
- Enforced storage-level upload policy during finalize/index in:
  - `apps/api/modules/documents/service.py`
    by validating:
  - actual stored object size
  - configured file-size ceiling
  - normalized MIME/type class
  - allowed extension family
  - expected MIME and declared size consistency
- Persisted validated storage metadata before indexing continues
- Applied authenticated document-surface throttling to:
  - signed upload URL issuance (`document_upload`)
  - finalize/index processing (`document_finalize`)
    in:
  - `apps/api/modules/documents/routes.py`
- Added explicit `429` response metadata to the throttled document routes
- Added dedicated HTTP status handling for:
  - oversize uploads
  - stored-object policy violations
- Confirmed storage object naming remains sanitized, UUID-scoped, non-overwriting, and under the controlled `official/...` object prefix

Acceptance:

- Oversized files cannot pass the effective upload pipeline merely by bypassing pre-issuance checks
- Upload request validation is tighter and policy-driven
- Upload endpoints are throttled according to sensitivity
- Finalize/index rejects objects that violate size or file-type policy
- User-supplied metadata is normalized consistently before persistence

### Pass S5: Frontend Security Review and Dependency Hygiene

Status:

- Implemented

Goal:

- Verify the frontend auth layer does not undermine backend protections and formalize dependency risk review.

Work:

- Audited auth-related frontend code for:
  - hardcoded secrets
  - unsafe token handling
  - insecure storage of sensitive data
- Verified the current frontend auth model does not store custom access or refresh tokens in homemade cookies/localStorage:
  - Supabase remains the session authority
  - sessionStorage usage in password setup/reset is limited to a non-secret route-access marker
  - admin API calls read the current Supabase access token at request time instead of persisting duplicate token state
- Hardened non-secret frontend cookie handling in:
  - `apps/web/src/lib/auth/preferences.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/components/ui/sidebar.tsx`
    by adding explicit `SameSite=Lax` handling everywhere and `Secure` on HTTPS origins
- Audited frontend dependency state with `npm audit`
- Updated frontend dependencies to remove known advisories:
  - upgraded `next` to `^16.2.1`
  - upgraded `eslint-config-next` to `^16.2.1`
  - added an `overrides` rule for `flatted` to `^3.4.2`
    in:
  - `apps/web/package.json`
  - `apps/web/package-lock.json`
- Audited backend dependency state with `pip-audit`
- Updated backend dependencies to remove patchable findings in:
  - `apps/api/requirements.txt`
    by upgrading:
  - `requests` to `2.33.0`
  - `pyasn1` to `0.6.3`
- Recorded the one remaining backend dependency risk:
  - `ecdsa==0.19.1` reports a Minerva timing-attack advisory with no upstream fix
  - current backend usage is limited to JWT verification flow, not ECDSA signing or key generation
  - keep/replace rationale: retain for now with explicit residual-risk note; consider migrating JWT verification away from the current dependency chain if the project later needs a stricter cryptography-only verification stack
- Reviewed the current public auth messaging tradeoff against the throttled backend posture and kept the explicit messaging model because:
  - auth-status probing is now rate-limited
  - abuse logging exists
  - no frontend secret storage was added to support that UX

Acceptance:

- Frontend auth code remains free of hardcoded secrets and unsafe token storage
- Dependency risk review is recorded with concrete recommendations and one residual no-fix backend crypto note
- Security-hardening phase has a verified closeout checklist

## Definition of Done For Security Phase

This security implementation phase is complete only when all of the following are true:

1. Backend rate limiting exists and is applied according to route sensitivity.
2. Rate limiting uses a production-safe identity/enforcement model and is not left as process-local best effort only.
3. Public auth-status probing is throttled and abuse-observable.
4. AI routes have explicit anti-abuse throttling.
5. Document upload issuance and finalize/index surfaces have explicit anti-abuse throttling.
6. Security headers are centrally enforced.
7. Backend origin / CORS policy is explicitly controlled per environment.
8. Upload validation includes a hard file-size ceiling and actual storage/finalize enforcement, not request-time checks only.
9. Auth-related input normalization is tightened without moving business logic out of PostgreSQL.
10. SQL injection review is explicitly completed for backend query paths handling user input.
11. XSS/output-sink review is explicitly completed for frontend and backend user-controlled text paths.
12. Frontend auth surfaces remain free of hardcoded secrets and unsafe token persistence.
13. Dependency review findings are documented with concrete keep/replace rationale.
14. Structured security logging exists for rate-limit and abuse-sensitive outcomes without leaking sensitive data.

# Admin User Management Gap-Closure Plan

## Purpose

This document section is the implementation plan for completing the missing admin user-management flow in the current backend and preparing a stable contract for the upcoming admin frontend module.

This is not a speculative feature note. It is based on the current tracked codebase and migrations as they exist now.

## Codebase Audit Snapshot

### Current admin user-management backend surface

The backend currently exposes these admin-user routes in `apps/api/modules/auth/routes.py`:

- `POST /api/admin/users/students`
- `POST /api/admin/users/librarians`
- `POST /api/admin/users/admins`
- `PATCH /api/admin/users/{user_id}/profile`
- `DELETE /api/admin/users/{user_id}`

The current service behavior in `apps/api/modules/auth/service.py` is:

- create users through the Supabase Admin API invite flow
- write role metadata into:
  - `library.students`
  - `library.librarians`
  - `library.admins`
- update only `library.profiles.full_name` through `library.admin_update_profile(...)`
- delete users through `library.admin_delete_user(...)`

### Current schema reality

The current tracked PostgreSQL schema shows:

- `library.profiles` currently has:
  - `id`
  - `full_name`
  - `role`
  - `created_at`
- `library.students` currently has:
  - `id`
  - `roll_number`
  - `department`
  - `semester`
  - `created_at`
- `library.librarians` currently has:
  - `id`
  - `employee_code`
  - `department`
  - `created_at`
- `library.admins` currently has:
  - `id`
  - `designation`
  - `created_at`

Important schema facts:

- there is no user-level `is_active` field yet
- there is no user-level `updated_at` field yet
- role metadata tables also do not currently expose `updated_at`
- uniqueness already exists for:
  - `students.roll_number`
  - `librarians.employee_code`

### Current account-state reality

The current auth/account-state implementation only distinguishes:

- active existing auth account
- deleted account marker in `library.deleted_accounts`

It does **not** currently support a third application-level state for inactive-but-not-deleted users.

Current evidence:

- `POST /api/auth/account-status` in `apps/api/modules/auth/service.py` checks:
  - `auth.users` existence
  - `library.is_account_deleted(email)`
- JWT middleware in `apps/api/core/middleware.py` validates identity only
- protected requests are not currently blocked by any application-level `is_active` user flag because that flag does not yet exist

## Corrections To The Prior Draft

The draft direction is broadly correct, but several assumptions were not true in the real codebase.

1. `is_active` is not already present for users.
   - It exists for deleted-account markers and document records.
   - It does not exist on `library.profiles` or role metadata tables.

2. `updated_at` is not already present for admin-managed users.
   - If list/detail responses promise `updated_at`, the schema and write paths must be extended first.

3. The current update route is much narrower than the draft implied.
   - `PATCH /api/admin/users/{user_id}/profile` currently accepts only `full_name`.
   - It calls `library.admin_update_profile(p_target_user_id, p_full_name)`.
   - It does not update role metadata today.

4. Activation/deactivation cannot be treated as a UI-only toggle.
   - Without an enforcement path in backend auth/account checks, a status endpoint would be fake.

5. One route helper already depends on an undocumented database function.
   - `apps/api/modules/auth/routes.py` calls `select library.is_admin($1);`
   - no tracked migration currently defines `library.is_admin(...)`
   - this must be verified during implementation so admin-route authorization is not built on invisible drift

## Locked Decisions For This Phase

1. Scope for this phase includes only:
   - admin user list
   - user detail read
   - search/filter support on user list
   - expanded update-user behavior by role
   - activate/deactivate user

2. This phase explicitly excludes:
   - role change
   - suspend/restore workflow separate from activate/deactivate
   - admin-frontend implementation beyond what the contract needs

3. FastAPI must remain thin.
   - query orchestration for list/detail is acceptable in the backend service layer
   - role validation and mutation integrity should live in PostgreSQL where it affects persistent rules

4. Email remains immutable in this phase.
   - admin update routes must reject email mutation attempts
   - changing auth email is identity work, not profile-edit work

5. Read responses must use one stable contract family across roles.
   - do not return unrelated shapes for student, librarian, and admin rows
   - use one stable object with nullable role-specific fields

6. The backend must expose optional server-side query parameters even if the first frontend pass uses cached local filtering.

## Target Contract Direction

### 1. List Users

Route:

- `GET /api/admin/users`

Purpose:

- return admin-manageable users in one stable list shape
- support future-safe query parameters even if the first frontend pass mostly refines cached results locally

Recommended query params:

- `role`
- `is_active`
- `search`
- `limit`
- `offset`
- optional `sort_by`
- optional `sort_order`

Planned response body:

- envelope remains:
  - `status`
  - `message`
  - `data`
  - `timestamp_ms`
- `data` should contain:
  - `items`
  - `total`
  - `limit`
  - `offset`

### 2. User Detail Read

Route:

- `GET /api/admin/users/{user_id}`

Purpose:

- return one complete admin-manageable user record for detail display and edit-form prefill

Response direction:

- reuse the same field family as list rows
- return one `user` object inside the standard envelope

### 3. Expanded Update User

Existing route to keep:

- `PATCH /api/admin/users/{user_id}/profile`

Expansion rules:

- backend reads target user role first
- backend validates allowed fields against that role
- backend updates `library.profiles` plus the correct role table atomically
- backend rejects irrelevant fields with `422`
- backend rejects any email update attempt with `422`

Allowed fields by role:

- student:
  - `full_name`
  - `roll_number`
  - `department`
  - `semester`
- librarian:
  - `full_name`
  - `employee_code`
  - `department`
- admin:
  - `full_name`
  - `designation`

### 4. Activate / Deactivate User

Recommended route:

- `PATCH /api/admin/users/{user_id}/status`

Request direction:

- `{"is_active": true|false}`

Meaning:

- deactivate keeps the user record and history
- delete still means account removal
- these actions must remain separate

## Stable Read Model Requirements

The admin frontend needs one stable read model. Without that, list, detail, cache updates, and local table refinement become brittle.

The required read model for this phase should include these common fields:

- `user_id`
- `email`
- `role`
- `is_active`
- `full_name`
- `created_at`

The required read model for this phase should include these nullable role-specific fields:

- `roll_number`
- `department`
- `semester`
- `employee_code`
- `designation`

Phase-scope rule:

- `updated_at` is **not** a required contract field for this phase.
- It may be added in a later phase only if the schema has one truthful and clearly defined source for that timestamp across profile and role-metadata writes.
- Do not expand this phase with profile/role timestamp trigger work only to satisfy a non-essential field.

Contract rule:

- API responses may expose uppercase role strings if needed for consistency with the existing create-user responses.
- database storage remains lowercase `library.role_enum`
- the backend must normalize this boundary intentionally instead of leaking mixed casing unpredictably

### Search / filter contract

The search behavior must be explicit, not implied.

For this phase:

- `search` matches case-insensitively against:
  - `email`
  - `full_name`
  - `roll_number`
  - `employee_code`
  - `department`
  - `designation`
- `role` filters exact role
- `is_active` filters account state
- pagination remains bounded by `limit` and `offset`
- `sort_by` and `sort_order` remain optional and should only be implemented now if the current frontend contract actually needs them

## Required Database And Auth Decisions

### Source of truth for activation state

This is the main design decision for the phase.

The implementation will adopt:

- `library.profiles.is_active boolean not null default true`

Reason:

- activation is an application-level usability flag
- it belongs to the shared user profile record, not to one role-specific table
- placing it only in metadata tables would fragment user-state logic
- placing it only in frontend cache would be meaningless

### Read-model implementation direction

The preferred implementation for list/detail is a database-backed admin read model:

- either a dedicated SQL view
- or a dedicated database query function / RPC if that fits the current migration style better

That read model should centralize the cross-schema merge across:

- `auth.users`
- `library.profiles`
- `library.students`
- `library.librarians`
- `library.admins`

Reason:

- keeps FastAPI thin
- keeps cross-schema merge rules in one place
- avoids leaking read-model assembly rules into Python
- better matches the locked DB-centric architecture

Fallback rule:

- a temporary parameterized service-layer read is acceptable only if the current codebase cannot support the DB-backed read model cleanly right now
- even then, FastAPI must remain limited to projection, parameter mapping, and response envelopes
- role-merge rules must not become scattered business logic in Python

## Activation Enforcement Requirements

Adding `PATCH /status` without real enforcement would be dishonest.

Deactivate must be enforced across normal protected application access, not only across admin-managed routes.

Minimum enforcement requirement for this phase:

- after JWT validation and user resolution, inactive accounts must be denied for protected business endpoints
- any exception must be explicit and minimal
- if an allowlist is needed, it should be limited to account-recovery style endpoints such as `account-status` and similar non-business auth checks

Contract direction:

- `POST /api/auth/account-status` should expose inactive-account state explicitly so the frontend can render a dedicated inactive-account outcome
- protected API behavior must not treat inactive users as normal active users

## Implementation Passes

### Pass A1: Admin User Management Audit Lock

Status:

- Implemented

Goal:

- lock the actual current admin-user contract and remove undocumented assumptions before behavior changes

Work:

- audited the current admin-user route surface in `apps/api/modules/auth/routes.py`
- audited the current admin-user service behavior in `apps/api/modules/auth/service.py`
- confirmed tracked schema coverage from the current migrations for:
  - `library.profiles`
  - `library.students`
  - `library.librarians`
  - `library.admins`
  - `library.deleted_accounts`
- verified that the current tracked schema still does **not** define:
  - user-level `is_active`
  - user-level `updated_at`
  - `library.is_admin(...)` in tracked migrations
- checked repository-local schema snapshots and found no tracked evidence that `library.is_admin(...)` exists outside the migrations committed in the repo
- confirmed the current frontend admin API layer expects backend-managed role strings and that existing create-user responses already use uppercase role values

Acceptance:

- admin-user implementation assumptions are tied to tracked schema or explicitly documented runtime drift

Audit findings:

- Current admin-user write routes exist, but read/list/status routes do not.
- `PATCH /api/admin/users/{user_id}/profile` still updates only `full_name`.
- `DELETE /api/admin/users/{user_id}` is real and is backed by deleted-account marker logic plus auth-user deletion.
- The route-layer admin guard currently depends on `select library.is_admin($1);`, but that helper is not defined in the tracked migrations or repository schema snapshot.
- The current repo does not provide evidence of additional tracked local schema drift beyond that undocumented helper gap.
- The current frontend admin API client already mirrors uppercase role examples on create-user responses, so future read endpoints should normalize role casing intentionally instead of leaking raw lowercase enum values.

### Pass A2: User State Schema Foundation

Status:

- Implemented

  - `default true`
  - `not null`

  - speculative search indexes whose query shape is not yet locked

Acceptance:

- user activation state exists as real persistent data instead of API fiction
- existing users remain valid after migration
- list/status APIs have a truthful shared source for activity state

Implementation note:

- This pass only establishes the persistent source of truth.
- It does not yet expose status APIs or protected-request enforcement.
- Those behavior changes remain in later passes so this phase stays disciplined instead of mixing schema and route logic carelessly.

### Pass A4: Role-Aware Update Expansion

Status:

- Implemented

  - `full_name`
  - `roll_number`
  - `department`
  - `semester`
  - `employee_code`
  - `designation`

  - `extra="forbid"`
  - field normalization

- enforced in the database path:
  - email remains immutable because it is not part of the update contract

  - validation for bounded text and semester/roll-number format
  - active-admin-only privilege check
