  - `node .\\apps\\web\\node_modules\\typescript\\bin\\tsc -p .\\apps\\web\\tsconfig.json --noEmit`
- pushed the new migration with `supabase db push`

Acceptance:

- admin can update role-relevant fields safely
- invalid cross-role payloads are rejected deterministically
- update integrity does not depend on scattered Python-side field branching

Implementation note:

- This pass expands only the role-aware update flow.
- It does not yet add activation/deactivation writes or inactive-user enforcement on protected business routes.

### Pass A5: Activation / Deactivation

Status:

- Implemented

Goal:

- implement real account activation-state management rather than a cosmetic flag

Work:

- created migration `supabase/migrations/20260326120854_library_admin_user_activation_enforcement.sql`
- added `PATCH /api/admin/users/{user_id}/status`
- added database-owned activation helpers:
  - `library.admin_set_user_active_status(...)`
  - `library.get_profile_access_context(...)`
- persisted activation changes in `library.profiles.is_active`
- added protected-request enforcement in `apps/api/core/account_access.py`
- registered the account-access middleware in `apps/api/main.py` so authenticated requests are blocked when:
  - the profile is missing
  - the account is inactive
- attached resolved `role` and `is_active` to request state for downstream use
- updated `POST /api/auth/account-status` so it now exposes inactive-account state explicitly through `is_inactive`
- updated the frontend guest-auth client in `apps/web/src/lib/api/auth.ts` so:
  - inactive accounts are surfaced during failed login checks
  - successful Supabase sign-in is immediately torn down if the app-level account is inactive
  - successful Supabase sign-in now also fails closed if account-state verification cannot be resolved, so a fresh auth session is never kept without a confirmed application-level access decision
  - password-reset requests also block inactive accounts with a deterministic message
- added the admin frontend API client contract for the new status endpoint in `apps/web/src/lib/api/admin.ts`
- verified backend Python syntax with `python -m compileall apps/api`
- verified frontend TypeScript compatibility with:
  - `node .\\apps\\web\\node_modules\\typescript\\bin\\tsc -p .\\apps\\web\\tsconfig.json --noEmit`
- pushed the new migration with `supabase db push`

Acceptance:

- deactivated users are denied normal protected application access
- reactivated users regain normal application usability
- inactive state is observable in a stable backend contract
- delete behavior remains separate and unaffected

Implementation note:

- This pass keeps delete and deactivate separate by design:
  - delete still removes the auth user and records the deleted-account marker
  - deactivate keeps the account record but blocks protected application use
- The sign-in flow needed one additional hardening correction after implementation review:
  - the second post-auth `account-status` check already existed
  - the real remaining gap was that an unresolved `account-status` lookup still allowed a freshly created Supabase session to survive
  - the frontend gate is now fail-closed after successful credential auth, which is stricter and more truthful than allowing an unverified application session

### Pass A6: Contract Finalization And Testing

Status:

- Implemented

Goal:

- freeze the admin-user API contract for frontend integration

Work:

- finalized the shared frontend admin API contract in `apps/web/src/lib/api/admin.ts` by adding:
  - admin-managed user row type
  - list response type
  - detail response type
  - list query options
  - `getAdminManagedUsers(...)`
  - `getAdminManagedUserById(...)`
  - existing create/update/status/delete helpers kept aligned with the backend envelope

  - `POST /api/auth/account-status`
  - `GET /api/admin/users`
  - `GET /api/admin/users/{user_id}`
  - `PATCH /api/admin/users/{user_id}/profile`
  - `PATCH /api/admin/users/{user_id}/status`
  - `DELETE /api/admin/users/{user_id}`
- verified that backend route schemas/examples now cover the admin-user surface:
  - list
  - detail
  - create

  - status update
  - delete

  - delete still calls `library.admin_delete_user(...)`

- performed non-test-file verification, per current project instruction, through:

- intentionally did **not** add automated test files because this phase was approved without test-file creation

Acceptance:

- admin-user contract is stable enough for frontend module implementation

Verification note:

- The expected matrix for this phase is now contract-covered and implementation-covered without creating test files:
  - list returns mixed roles correctly
  - detail returns correct role metadata
  - search/filter parameters are defined
  - invalid role-field mismatches map to `422`
  - email updates remain outside the schema and are therefore rejected by contract
  - duplicate `roll_number` maps to `409`
  - duplicate `employee_code` maps to `409`
  - deactivate/reactivate path exists and is enforced
  - delete behavior remains separate and unaffected

## Known Risks To Watch During Coding

1. Hidden database drift may exist around admin authorization.
   - Resolved during this phase.
   - `library.is_admin(...)` is now defined in tracked migrations.

2. Activation can become fake if enforcement is not wired into a real access path.

3. Read-model responses can become inconsistent if role casing is not normalized deliberately at the API boundary.

4. `updated_at` can become misleading if the plan promises one field without defining whether it comes from profile-only mutation time or merged profile/role-metadata mutation time.

5. Search can become expensive if it is added without indexes or a bounded default pagination policy.

6. Expanding update behavior in Python alone would violate the current architecture if it starts replacing database-owned mutation rules with route/service business logic.

## Definition of Done For Admin User Management Phase

This phase is complete only when all of the following are true:

1. Admins can list users through `GET /api/admin/users`.
2. Admins can read one user through `GET /api/admin/users/{user_id}`.
3. The list/detail contract uses one stable cross-role response shape.
4. The existing update route supports role-aware metadata updates instead of only `full_name`.
5. Email mutation is rejected explicitly.
6. Activate/deactivate exists as a real backend-enforced account state.
7. Delete remains separate from deactivate and still works.
8. Search/filter/pagination inputs are validated strictly.
9. Response shapes are documented and stable for frontend consumption.
10. Any undocumented admin-authorization dependency discovered during implementation is either migrated properly or removed.

## Post-Phase Correction: Admin Create-Flow Integrity

This correction is required because the currently implemented create flow can still leave partial users behind when role-metadata uniqueness fails after the auth user has already been created.

Verified current runtime sequence:

1. invite/create the auth user in Supabase
2. update Supabase metadata so the `library.profiles` trigger materializes the profile row
3. insert role metadata into `library.students`, `library.librarians`, or `library.admins`
4. if a role-specific unique constraint fails at step 3, the auth user and base profile already exist

Verified failure evidence:

- `apps/api/modules/auth/service.py`
  - `create_student(...)`, `create_librarian(...)`, and `create_admin(...)` currently create/finalize the Supabase user before inserting role metadata
- `apps/api/app.log`
  - the logged student failure shows:
    - auth user metadata was updated successfully
    - then `students_roll_number_key` failed
    - then later detail reads failed with `Student profile metadata missing`

Important correction:

- preflight uniqueness validation is necessary, but it is not sufficient by itself
- two concurrent requests could still pass the same preflight check and race into the final insert
- so the fix must include both:
  - database-backed preflight validation before auth-user creation
  - compensating cleanup if post-auth persistence still fails after creation

### Pass A7: Admin Create-Flow Integrity Hardening

Status:

- Implemented

Goal:

- prevent create-user requests from leaving partial auth/profile records behind when role-specific metadata is invalid or conflicts with existing unique values

Work:

- added tracked migration `supabase/migrations/20260327170500_library_admin_create_flow_integrity.sql`
  - added `library.validate_admin_user_creation_metadata(...)`
  - added `library.cleanup_failed_provisioned_user(...)`
- updated `apps/api/modules/auth/service.py`
  - create flows now run database-backed metadata preflight before any auth-user creation
  - late role-metadata persistence failures now map deterministically for `roll_number` and `employee_code`
  - if a create flow fails after the auth user has been provisioned, the service now deletes that just-created auth user through the tracked cleanup function so the profile and dependent rows cascade away
  - cleanup can resolve the created auth user either by known `user_id` or, if needed, by normalized email lookup
- updated `apps/api/modules/auth/routes.py`
  - create endpoints now document `409` for email or unique role-metadata conflicts
  - create endpoints now document `422` for invalid role metadata payloads
  - runtime error mapping now treats `Invalid role` as `422`
- verified the live preflight behavior directly against the database after migration push
  - duplicate student roll number `2022-ag-9159` now fails at the preflight validator with `Roll number already exists`
- intentionally did **not** add a blind hardcoded cleanup migration for previously orphaned partial users
  - historical broken records must be reviewed intentionally instead of being deleted speculatively

Acceptance:

- duplicate student `roll_number` is rejected before a new auth user is left behind
- duplicate librarian `employee_code` is rejected before a new auth user is left behind
- late race-condition conflicts still do not leave partial users behind because compensating cleanup removes the just-created auth user
- create endpoints return deterministic `409` responses for role-metadata uniqueness conflicts
- successful create behavior remains unchanged for valid payloads

# Next Frontend Phase Plan

## Purpose

This section locks the next coding phase after the backend-heavy admin-user-management work.

This next phase has three linked concerns:

1. fix the current login sequencing bug so the dashboard opens only after both the Supabase sign-in call and the backend `account-status` call have completed successfully
2. refine the admin user-detail contract without breaking the stable mixed-role list contract
3. build the authenticated shared shell and the first admin user-management screens inside that shell

This is a planning lock only. No implementation is considered complete until the respective passes are coded, verified, and then marked implemented.

## Current Repo Findings

### 1. Login sequencing is still logically broken

The current frontend already performs a second `account-status` check after successful Supabase password sign-in.

That means the old problem statement of Ã¢â‚¬Å“the second check does not existÃ¢â‚¬Â is no longer true.

The real remaining bug is different:

- `supabase.auth.signInWithPassword(...)` succeeds
- the client auth provider receives `SIGNED_IN`
- guest-route redirect logic can start dashboard redirection from raw authenticated session state
- this can happen before the controlled login submit flow finishes its full application-level `account-status` verification path

So the current app has two redirect authorities:

- the explicit login submit flow
- the global auth-state listener / guest-route redirect flow

That is weak because the dashboard route can begin opening from bare Supabase session truth before the login flow finishes its backend-owned account-state check.

Locked correction:

- clicking login must follow this exact order:
  - call Supabase password login
  - call backend `POST /api/auth/account-status`
  - only after both succeed, finalize auth refresh and open the role dashboard

### 2. The authenticated role layouts are still only guard wrappers

Current role layouts in:

- `apps/web/src/app/(admin)/admin/layout.tsx`
- `apps/web/src/app/(librarian)/librarian/layout.tsx`
- `apps/web/src/app/(student)/student/layout.tsx`

currently do only this:

- fetch current server user
- apply server-side role redirect guard
- return `children`

That means the repo does not yet have a real authenticated shell.

### 3. Current dashboards are placeholders, not shell-backed modules

The dashboard roots currently render `RoleDashboardPlaceholder` inside `ProtectedRouteShell`.

That placeholder was acceptable for auth migration stabilization, but it is not an acceptable long-term base for:

- admin navigation
- page header behavior
- avatar/user menu
- responsive sidebar
- role-specific shell configuration

### 4. The repo already contains real shell building blocks

The current frontend is not starting from zero.

The repo already has:

- typography and color tokens in `apps/web/src/app/globals.css`
- the same core visual language as the sample:
  - Public Sans display font
  - Roboto body font
  - light-slate surfaces
  - purple primary
  - blue accent
- a reusable sidebar primitive in `apps/web/src/components/ui/sidebar.tsx`
- toast infrastructure
- animation helpers such as `ScrollReveal`

So the shell implementation should reuse the current system instead of inventing a parallel layout stack.

### 5. Admin frontend contract groundwork already exists

The admin API client already exposes:

- dashboard metrics
- user list
- user detail
- create user
- update user profile
- update user status
- delete user

But the current admin detail client type still mirrors the flat mixed-role object shape.

That is acceptable for the list view, but it is not the cleanest shape for the detail screen and role-specific edit UI.

### 6. No admin user-management screens exist yet

The repo currently has no real admin users module pages or components.

So the next phase must build:

- shell first
- admin screens second

Trying to do screens first would create layout churn and duplicated shell logic.

## Locked Decisions For This Phase

### 1. Login flow sequence is now fixed as a contract

For the login screen, the only valid success sequence is:

1. user clicks sign in
2. frontend calls Supabase password sign-in
3. frontend calls backend `POST /api/auth/account-status`
4. frontend validates the result
5. frontend refreshes normalized auth state intentionally
6. frontend opens the correct role dashboard

Rules:

- the login page must not redirect to a dashboard from raw `SIGNED_IN` state while the controlled login handshake is still pending
- successful Supabase auth alone is not enough to open the app
- the app must not keep two competing redirect paths during login submit
- account-status failure after successful credential auth must still fail closed

### 2. Admin list contract stays stable

`GET /api/admin/users` remains one stable mixed-role list shape.

It will keep:

- common fields
- nullable role-specific fields
- one stable cached row model for local filtering, sorting, and pagination rendering

This must not be changed into a union of unrelated role row objects.

### 3. Admin detail contract becomes role-explicit

`GET /api/admin/users/{user_id}` will be refined for detail-screen truth.

Direction:

- keep common top-level fields such as:
  - `user_id`
  - `email`
  - `role`
  - `is_active`
  - `full_name`
  - `created_at`
- replace the flat nullable detail payload with one role-explicit nested object:
  - `student_profile`
  - `librarian_profile`
  - `admin_profile`

Contract rule:

- only the matching role profile object should be present
- the non-matching role profile objects should not be fabricated

Why:

- list caching stays simple
- detail rendering becomes cleaner
- edit-form field ownership becomes explicit

### 4. The shared shell must be role-agnostic

The shell components must not hardcode admin-only behavior.

The shell system will be configured by role-specific layout wrappers through props and role nav data.

The shared shell will own:

- sidebar state
- topbar structure
- mobile drawer behavior
- layout composition

The role layouts will own:

- role nav map
- dashboard target
- role-scoped menu items
- role-scoped labels

### 5. The shell must follow the sample design language

The design direction is locked from `Sample Design Components.html`.

The shell must visually inherit:

- Public Sans + Roboto typography pairing
- light-slate surfaces and strong white cards
- purple primary emphasis with blue interactive accent
- rounded navigation items
- clear active-state treatment
- clean spacing and compact left-nav rhythm

This does not mean copying raw HTML.
It means implementing the same visual language through the current React component system and existing theme tokens.

### 6. The existing sidebar primitive must be reused

The repo already has `apps/web/src/components/ui/sidebar.tsx`.

So the shell plan will build on top of that primitive instead of replacing it with an unrelated custom sidebar implementation.

### 7. UX state coverage is mandatory

Per `AGENT.md`, the admin shell and admin screens must explicitly support:

- loading states
- empty states
- retry states
- disabled states
- permission-denied behavior
- destructive confirmation behavior
- stale-data refresh behavior
- truthful success and failure toasts

These are not optional polish items. They are part of the implementation contract.

### 8. No frontend-only security fiction

This phase must keep security aligned with the locked architecture.

That means:

- role access remains backend and SSR guarded
- the shell must consume existing auth truth instead of inventing parallel auth logic
- user-management screens must not assume API success without backend confirmation
- status and delete actions must use explicit confirmation and real mutation responses

## Design Translation Notes From The Sample

The sample is useful, but it is only a visual reference.

The actual coding plan will translate these ideas into the current repo:

- sidebar items should use rounded active pills with stronger contrast than idle items
- idle sidebar items should still have hover feedback and icon-color response
- topbar and sidebar must feel part of one system, not two unrelated bars
- page content should use layered surfaces:
  - soft page background
  - white or elevated cards
  - clear section spacing
- mobile behavior must become a real drawer, not a squeezed desktop sidebar

What will not be copied blindly:

- raw sample HTML
- external CDN styling patterns
- Material Symbols dependency

The repo already uses React, Lucide icons, shadcn-style primitives, and theme tokens.
The implementation must stay coherent with that stack.

## Required Output Areas For The Next Coding Phase

The next coding phase must cover these areas in order:

1. login sequencing correction
2. admin detail contract refinement
3. shared authenticated shell foundation
4. role layout integration
5. admin user-management frontend screens

### Post-Pass F1 Correction: Guest-Route Redirect Loop Hardening

Status:

- Implemented

Goal:

- stop the `/login` to protected-dashboard redirect loop that appeared when client-only auth state disagreed with server-side protected-route resolution

Runtime truth:

- the loop happened because the guest login screen still trusted hydrated client auth state enough to redirect to a role dashboard
- at the same time, the protected server layout could still resolve the request as unauthenticated and send the browser back to `/login`
- that disagreement created a ping-pong between guest and protected routes

Work:

- updated `apps/web/src/components/providers/auth-provider.tsx`
  - stopped trusting `INITIAL_SESSION` blindly
  - revalidated initial client auth state with `supabase.auth.getUser()` before accepting the browser session as authenticated
- updated `apps/web/src/lib/auth/route-state.ts`
  - added a guest-route option to respect a server-approved guest render instead of forcing a client-only authenticated redirect
- updated:
  - `apps/web/src/components/auth/login-screen.tsx`
  - `apps/web/src/components/auth/forgot-password-screen.tsx`
    so these guest screens now respect the fact that the server already chose to render them instead of overriding that decision with a client-only redirect

Acceptance:

- guest screens no longer bounce repeatedly into protected role routes when the client store and server guard disagree
- protected layouts remain server-authoritative
- explicit post-login navigation still works through the controlled submit flow

## Frontend Hook And Module Structure Remediation Plan

This section corrects a structural mistake in the current frontend organization.

The current admin users module works functionally, but its file system shape violates the now-locked frontend convention in `AGENT.md`:

- module API logic must remain in one module API file such as `admin.ts`
- module hook/state logic must remain in one module hook file such as `useAdmin.ts`
- hook filenames must use `use` + PascalCase
- module-specific hook folders must not be used unless explicitly approved

That means the current structure under `apps/web/src/hooks/admin-users/` is no longer acceptable as the source-of-truth organization.

### Current Structural Problems

The current repo contains these convention mismatches:

- module-specific hook folder:
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/admin-users/`
- kebab-case hook filenames:
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/use-app-auth.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/use-auth-session-actions.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/use-guest-route-state.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/use-mobile.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/admin-users/use-admin-user-detail.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/admin-users/use-admin-users-list.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/admin-users/use-admin-users-mutation-feedback.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/admin-users/use-admin-users-tabs-view.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/admin-users/use-create-admin-managed-user.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/admin-users/use-delete-admin-user.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/admin-users/use-update-admin-user-profile.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/admin-users/use-update-admin-user-status.ts`
- split admin module state across many small files even though the approved module rule requires one module hook file

This is not just a naming issue.

It is also an ownership and maintainability issue because one module now spreads its cache truth, selectors, mutation handling, and table state across too many files. That increases import churn, weakens discoverability, and makes the module harder to audit.

### Locked Remediation Direction

The correction direction is now fixed:

- keep `apps/web/src/lib/api/admin.ts` as the single admin API file
- consolidate admin-module hook/state logic into:
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/useAdmin.ts`
- migrate the current admin-users module folder content into that single hook file:
  - list fetch and cached refinement
  - detail fetch
  - shared cache/store state
  - create/update/status/delete mutations
  - mutation feedback and session recovery handling
  - tab/list view state derived from the cached list
- rename generic shared hooks to PascalCase:
  - `use-app-auth.ts` -> `useAppAuth.ts`
  - `use-auth-session-actions.ts` -> `useAuthSessionActions.ts`
  - `use-guest-route-state.ts` -> `useGuestRouteState.ts`
  - `use-mobile.ts` -> `useMobile.ts`
- remove the `apps/web/src/hooks/admin-users/` folder after all imports are migrated

### Important Non-Goals

This remediation must not pretend to be a feature pass.

It must not:

- change backend contracts
- redesign admin user screen behavior
- change create/update/status/delete semantics
- introduce a second admin API file
- silently change shell navigation behavior

The purpose is structural correctness, not feature expansion.

## Admin Users List Pagination And Dataset Scaling Plan

This section records the next planning phase for the admin users list screen after the user manually refined the current list layout and control styling.

The current screen and its subcomponents are now the visual source of truth for future list-screen changes. That means the next implementation must extend the existing table-card, tab, toolbar, and control styling instead of reintroducing older decorative layouts.

## Current Runtime Truth

The concern about the frontend stopping after the first 100 users is not currently true.

The present frontend implementation already aggregates more than one backend page:

- `apps/web/src/lib/api/admin.ts`
  - `getAllAdminManagedUsers(...)` repeatedly calls `GET /api/admin/users` with `limit=100` and increasing `offset`
- `apps/web/src/hooks/useAdmin.ts`
  - `useAdminUsersTabsView(...)` consumes that aggregated cache and performs local tab filtering, search, and pagination on the combined in-memory result set

So the real current state is:

- the frontend does not stop at 100 users
- the frontend currently uses repeated offset-based full-directory loading
- the current user-facing gaps are UI and pagination-control related, not a hard failure to load user 101

That means next-cursor support is a possible scalability improvement, but not a required fix for a currently broken list.

## Goals

The next implementation phase must do all of the following without breaking the current design language:

- add a rows-per-page control
- replace the current large-screen `Page X of Y` readout with page buttons in the same control family as the chevron buttons
- keep mobile pagination visually simple and consistent with the current compact control design
- remove hardcoded pagination assumptions from the table logic
- document the backend pagination strategy truthfully before changing API contracts

## Locked Design Direction

The next passes must preserve the current admin users list design that is now visible in the repo:

- the page header stays lean and direct
- the main list stays inside one restrained rounded table card
- search and status filter stay in the current compact toolbar arrangement
- tabs stay compact and badge-backed
- new pagination and row-count controls must visually match the current control family already used in the screen

## Planning Decision On Cursor Pagination

Cursor pagination should not be added under the false claim that the current list breaks after 100 users.

The correct decision is:

- first fix the current UI and pagination-state gaps on top of the existing aggregated offset strategy
- then decide whether backend cursor pagination is worth adding as a deliberate contract evolution for scale and consistency

This keeps the plan honest.

## Planned Passes

### Pass UL1: Current Pagination Truth And Design Lock

Status:

- Implemented

Goal:

- freeze the truthful baseline before code changes

Work:

- record that the current frontend already loads beyond 100 users through repeated offset requests
- record that the current admin users list design is authoritative and must be preserved
- record that cursor support is optional future evolution, not the fix for a current hard cap
- verified the current implementation points that make this true:
  - `apps/web/src/lib/api/admin.ts`
    - `getAllAdminManagedUsers(...)` continues loading with `limit` + `offset` until the backend returns the full current directory window
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/useAdmin.ts`
    - `useAdminUsersTabsView(...)` paginates locally on top of that aggregated cache
  - `apps/web/src/components/admin-users/admin-users-list-screen.tsx`
    - the current screen design was reviewed and locked as the source-of-truth interaction pattern for the next UI passes
- documented the corresponding continuity rules in:
  - `AGENT.md`
    - `Frontend Design Continuity Rules`

Acceptance:

- the plan reflects the real pagination/data-loading behavior
- the design authority of the current user list screen is documented

- destructive user actions belong in the table row action surface, not inside the edit dialog

- pagination and rows controls stay inline on small screens instead of stacking into a second bulky footer rhythm
- dialogs remain scrollable but hide visible scrollbars by default
- canonical Tailwind utility classes should be used when an equivalent utility already exists

## Fine Management Module Plan

This section records the implementation plan for the fine-management module after auditing the real backend surface, database RPCs, and current frontend state.

This is a planning section, not an implementation record yet.

## Current Implemented Truth

The current fine module already exists and is not speculative.

### Backend routes that already exist

- self-service fine reads:
  - `GET /api/me/fines`
  - `GET /api/me/fines/history`
- staff fine reads:
  - `GET /api/admin/fines`
  - `GET /api/admin/fines/{fine_id}`
- settlement actions:
  - `POST /api/fines/{fine_id}/pay`
  - `POST /api/fines/{fine_id}/waive`

### Actual permission behavior from the audited SQL/RPC layer

- admin fine reads are not actually admin-only
- `library._require_history_reader()` allows both:
  - `librarian`
  - `admin`
- fine settlement actions are also not admin-only
- `library._require_fine_manager()` allows both:
  - `librarian`
  - `admin`

So the current backend contract is really a staff fine-management surface exposed under an admin-prefixed read route plus generic fine action routes.

### Current contract strengths

- fine calculation remains database-owned
- pay and waive are already database-owned RPC actions
- invalid settlement states are already guarded in PostgreSQL:
  - `Fine not found`
  - `Fine already settled`
  - `Fine is not pending`
- detailed fine reads already expose issue/due/return timing data

### Current contract gaps

- `GET /api/admin/fines` only supports:
  - `status`
  - `limit`
  - `offset`
- `GET /api/admin/fines` response data currently returns only:
  - `items`
- there is no stable list metadata block for:
  - `total`
  - `limit`
  - `offset`
- there is no current search support for:
  - borrower name
  - book title
  - fine identifier
- there are no current created/resolved date filters
- fine routes currently do not apply dedicated route-level rate limiting
- there is no frontend fine module yet

### Current frontend truth

- there is no existing admin or librarian fine-management screen
- there is no current `apps/web/src/lib/api/fines.ts`
- there is no current `apps/web/src/hooks/useFines.ts`
- the current `apps/web/src/lib/api/admin.ts` does not include fine helpers
- student navigation already contains a disabled `Fines` entry
- admin and librarian authenticated navigation do not yet expose a fine-management entry

## Locked Planning Decisions

These decisions are fixed for the upcoming fine-management work.

1. Do not add a raw fine-amount edit API.
2. Do not move fine calculation or settlement rules into FastAPI.
3. The first backend blocker is the admin fine list contract, not settlement logic.
4. Staff fine list loading now follows a cached directory flow:
   - fetch the full fine directory into the module cache on first load
   - derive search, status, date filters, and pagination locally from cached rows
   - re-fetch only on explicit refresh or after mutation invalidation
   - when filtering one date field such as `fine_created_at`, use one date-range picker state with `from` and `to` values instead of separate date inputs
5. The frontend module boundary should be:
   - `apps/web/src/lib/api/fines.ts`
   - `apps/web/src/hooks/useFines.ts`
6. The frontend API layer may hide the current route inconsistency between:
   - `/api/admin/fines/...`
   - `/api/fines/{fine_id}/...`
7. Mandatory waive reasons must not be smuggled in as an assumed rule.
   - The current database contract allows a null or blank waive reason after trimming.
   - If mandatory waive reasons are desired later, that is an explicit contract change and migration, not a silent documentation tweak.
8. The first frontend delivery is staff fine management, not student self-service fines.

## Goals

The next implementation phase must do all of the following:

- strengthen the staff fine list contract for real server-driven table behavior
- support one cached full-directory fine fetch with truthful local refinement
- preserve database-owned settlement logic
- expose truthful pagination metadata
- keep settlement error semantics explicit enough for safe UI handling
- create one clean frontend module boundary for fine APIs and fine state
- deliver a first real staff fine-management screen without inventing unsupported backend behavior

## Planned Passes

### Pass FM-A1: Fine Contract Audit Lock

Status:

- Implemented

Goal:

- freeze the audited fine-module truth before code changes

Work:

- record the real currently shipped routes:
  - `GET /api/me/fines`
  - `GET /api/me/fines/history`
  - `GET /api/admin/fines`
  - `GET /api/admin/fines/{fine_id}`
  - `POST /api/fines/{fine_id}/pay`
  - `POST /api/fines/{fine_id}/waive`
- record the real permission truth from PostgreSQL:
  - staff fine reads are `librarian/admin`
  - settlement actions are `librarian/admin`
- record the current list-query limitations:
  - `status`
  - `limit`
  - `offset`
- record the current list-response limitation:
  - `data.items` only
- record the current waive-reason truth:
  - optional, not mandatory
- record that no frontend fine-management module exists yet

Acceptance:

- the plan no longer assumes false admin-only or required-reason behavior
- frontend work can proceed against the real audited contract

### Pass FM-A2: Staff Fine List Query Expansion

Status:

- Implemented

Goal:

- make `GET /api/admin/fines` strong enough for a real staff management table without leaving query semantics ambiguous

Work:

- add a migration that upgrades the database-backed staff fine list read path used by `GET /api/admin/fines`
- expand query support for:
  - `status`
  - `search`
  - `limit`
  - `offset`
  - optional `created_from`
  - optional `created_to`
  - optional `resolved_from`
  - optional `resolved_to`
- lock query semantics explicitly:
  - `search` is case-insensitive against:
    - borrower full name
    - book title
  - exact fine-id match may be supported only when the search term parses as a valid UUID
  - `created_*` filters apply to `fine_created_at`
  - `resolved_*` filters apply to `resolved_at` and only affect rows that have a non-null `resolved_at`
  - `created_from` and `resolved_from` are inclusive lower bounds
  - `created_to` and `resolved_to` are exclusive upper bounds
- keep FastAPI thin by passing the query contract through to the SQL/RPC layer instead of rebuilding filter logic in Python

Acceptance:

- the staff fine list supports truthful fine-directory refinement without ambiguous filter semantics
- search and date filtering are database-owned, not frontend-invented
- query behavior is explicit enough that backend and frontend cannot drift on filter meaning

- mount the shared fine-management screen under both protected role trees:
  - `apps/web/src/app/(admin)/admin/fines/page.tsx`
  - `apps/web/src/app/(librarian)/librarian/fines/page.tsx`
- reuse the same underlying fine-module screen/component instead of duplicating UI by role
- add authenticated-shell navigation entries for:
  - admin
  - librarian
- keep student `Fines` navigation disabled during this staff-management pass because student self-service fines are separate scope
- preserve the existing server-owned role guards and do not move access logic into the client shell

Acceptance:

- the shipped fine-management screen is reachable by both supported staff roles
- admin and librarian see the same truthful module behavior through their own protected shells
- navigation exposes only routes that actually exist in this phase

### Pass FM-X1: Fine RPC Auth-Context Correction

Status:

- typing in search or changing fine filters does not make another backend list request
- explicit refresh and post-mutation invalidation still reload backend truth when needed

## Blockers And Corrections

1. The first backend blocker is the list contract, not fine calculation.
2. Raw fine-amount editing remains out of scope and architecturally incorrect.
3. The fine screen now uses one cached directory load plus local refinement, so filter and search changes must not trigger repeated backend list requests.
4. Mandatory waive reasons are not part of the current contract and must not be assumed.
5. The clean frontend module boundary for this work is `fines.ts` plus `useFines.ts`, not more growth inside `admin.ts`.

## Admin Document Management Plan

This section records the implementation plan for the admin/staff document-management module after auditing the real backend routes, document ingestion pipeline, storage integration, current shell exposure, and current frontend state.

This is a planning section, not an implementation record yet.

## Current Audited Truth

The document module already exists and is operational. It is not a speculative feature.

### Backend routes that already exist

- document upload intent:
  - `POST /api/admin/documents/upload-url`
- document finalize/index:
  - `POST /api/admin/documents/{document_id}/finalize`
- document reads:
  - `GET /api/admin/documents`
  - `GET /api/admin/documents/{document_id}`
- document delete:
  - `DELETE /api/admin/documents/{document_id}`

### Actual permission behavior from the audited service layer

- the route prefix is admin-prefixed, but the actual permission rule is not admin-only
- `apps/api/modules/documents/service.py` allows both:
  - `admin`
  - `librarian`
- there is no student document-management access path

So the real backend contract is a staff document-management surface exposed under `/api/admin/documents/...`.

### Current contract strengths

- upload security is already partially hardened in shipped code:
  - filename normalization exists
  - file extension allowlist exists
  - MIME type allowlist/prefix enforcement exists
  - configured max file size is enforced at schema and service boundaries
  - storage object paths are server-generated under a controlled `official/...` prefix
  - signed upload URLs are generated server-side with `upsert: false`
- upload and finalize already have route-sensitive rate limits:
  - `document_upload`
  - `document_finalize`
- finalize validates the stored object before indexing:
  - object existence
  - actual stored size
  - actual stored MIME type
  - declared size and MIME consistency
- indexing is backend-owned through `DocumentIngestionService`
- only `indexed` documents are used by retrieval RPCs
- delete removes the storage object and deleting the document row cascades chunk cleanup through the database foreign key
- the current ingestion path already recognizes:
  - `uploaded`
  - `processing`
  - `indexed`
  - `failed`

### Current contract gaps or weak points

- `GET /api/admin/documents` still returns only:
  - `items`
- the list response has no stable pagination metadata:
  - `total`
  - `limit`
  - `offset`
- the list route currently has no query support for:
  - search
  - processing status filter
  - document type filter
  - audience scope filter
  - department filter
  - date filters
- list, detail, and delete currently do not apply dedicated route-level rate limiting
- route metadata is still under-documented for:
  - `401`
  - `403`
  - `404`
  - `409`
  - `422`
  - `429`
  - `500`
- a signed read/preview/download endpoint now exists:
  - `GET /api/admin/documents/{document_id}/read-url`
- document detail exposes storage metadata:
  - `bucket_name`
  - `storage_object_path`
  - `file_path`
    but those fields still must not be treated as raw frontend file URLs because the bucket remains private
- provisional uploaded rows now have an explicit stale-handling policy, but there is still no background cleanup automation in this phase

### Current lifecycle truth from the audited backend

- `upload-url` creates the database row immediately and sets:
  - `processing_status = 'uploaded'`
    even before the file is confirmed to exist in storage
- `finalize` first verifies that the storage object exists and matches policy
- if the storage object is missing, finalize returns conflict-style failure and the row remains `uploaded`
- if the object is valid, finalize moves the row to:
  - `processing`
    then either:
  - `indexed`
  - `failed`
- failed indexing is already recoverable through the same finalize path because the current ingestion service deletes old chunks and rebuilds them during re-finalization
- there is no separate reindex endpoint today, but there is an effective retry path through `finalize`
- this phase now locks a staff-visible stale-upload policy:
  - uploaded rows older than the configured grace window are treated as stale
  - stale uploaded rows remain visible for staff review and delete/re-upload handling
  - stale uploaded rows do not expose finalize as a valid action anymore
- finalize is now explicitly idempotent for already indexed documents:
  - it returns success without reprocessing
- finalize now rejects these conflict states explicitly:
  - upload intent expired and re-upload is required
  - document is already processing

### Current storage/read truth

- the storage service is explicitly for a private bucket
- backend indexing downloads private objects with the service-role key
- signed read URL issuance now exists for frontend preview/download:
  - `GET /api/admin/documents/{document_id}/read-url`
- preview/download is now valid only through backend-issued short-lived signed URLs, not raw storage paths

### Current frontend truth

- there is no current document management screen
- there is no current:
  - `apps/web/src/lib/api/documents.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/useDocuments.ts`
- admin shell navigation already includes a disabled `Documents` entry
- librarian shell navigation does not yet expose a documents entry even though backend auth currently allows librarian access

## Locked Planning Decisions

These decisions are fixed for the upcoming document-management work.

1. Do not move indexing, chunking, or retrieval-integrity logic into FastAPI or the frontend.
2. Do not fake preview/download from raw storage object paths.
3. Upload security hardening is not the primary missing area anymore because important parts already exist in shipped code.
4. The unresolved backend decision with the highest frontend impact is the read/preview contract, not upload intent creation.
5. The initial document list model will use a cached directory flow unless a stronger server-driven list contract is explicitly added first.
6. The frontend module boundary should be:
   - `apps/web/src/lib/api/documents.ts`
   - `apps/web/src/hooks/useDocuments.ts`
7. The document-management screen is a staff module, not an admin-only module, because the real backend permission rule currently allows both admin and librarian.
8. If preview/download is needed in this phase, it must be provided by a backend-issued short-lived signed read URL path.
9. If preview/download is not added in this phase, the module must remain metadata-management only and the UI must say that truthfully.
10. Failed documents already have a practical retry path through the existing finalize route, so a separate reindex endpoint is optional, not automatically required.

## Goals

The next implementation phase must do all of the following:

- freeze the real document contract so frontend work does not guess
- harden the remaining weak route-contract areas
- decide the read/preview contract explicitly
- choose one truthful list data model
- preserve RAG index integrity on finalize and delete flows
- create one clean frontend API boundary and one clean hook boundary
- deliver a real staff document-management module without inventing unsupported backend behavior

## Planned Passes

  - `DELETE /api/admin/documents/{document_id}`

  - document-management access is `admin/librarian`

  - upload intent creates an `uploaded` row before file existence is confirmed

### Pass DM-A2: Document Route And Upload-Security Contract Hardening

Status:

- Implemented

Goal:

- make the document route contract explicit enough for safe frontend handling, while tightening the remaining upload-trust gaps instead of assuming upload security is already fully solved

Work:

- document and normalize route responses for:
  - `401`
  - `403`
  - `404`
  - `409`
  - `422`
  - `429`
  - `500`
- apply this especially to:
  - upload-url
  - finalize
  - list
  - detail
  - delete
- add missing `429` response metadata where throttling is or will be applied
- remove ambiguity around conflict conditions such as:
  - missing storage object during finalize
  - missing document during detail/delete/finalize
- tighten the remaining upload-trust rules explicitly:
  - do not trust only the client-declared MIME type
  - do not trust only the stored object metadata MIME type
  - require agreement across the allowed extension policy, declared MIME policy, and stored-object validation path
  - where feasible for the currently allowed file types, verify the file signature / parser-recognized type during finalize before indexing proceeds
- ensure validation/error responses do not leak sensitive bucket internals, storage credentials, or implementation-only object details

Acceptance:

- frontend can distinguish permission failure, validation failure, conflict, throttling, and server failure truthfully
- upload security is not overstated in planning
- the remaining file-type trust boundary is explicitly hardened instead of being treated as already solved

### Pass DM-A4: Document List Model Decision

Status:

- Implemented

Goal:

- choose one truthful data-loading model for the document directory

Work:

Option A:

- keep the current backend list surface as a full-directory read
- implement a cached directory flow in the frontend
- load the full document directory once and derive search/filter/pagination locally

Option B:

- strengthen the backend list contract first with:
  - search
  - processing status
  - document type
  - audience scope
  - department
  - optional date filters
  - `total`
  - `limit`
  - `offset`

Current judgment:

- Option A is acceptable initially for this project because the current list route is thin and a cached staff-management table is already an allowed project pattern

Implemented decision:

- Option A is now the locked model for this phase
- the frontend document directory will:
  - load the full document directory once through the module API
  - keep the dataset in the module hook cache
  - derive search, filters, status refinement, and pagination locally
- the backend list contract will remain unchanged in this phase:
  - no server-driven search params
  - no server-driven filter params
  - no `total` / `limit` / `offset` response expansion
- later frontend screen copy and hook behavior must reflect cached-directory truth instead of implying server-driven filtering

Acceptance:

- the module no longer has an ambiguous list-loading strategy
- later frontend work can implement cached local refinement without pretending the backend supports server-driven search/filter pagination

### Pass DM-A6: Processing-State Integrity, Retry, And Provisional-Row Cleanup

Status:

- Implemented

Goal:

- make the document lifecycle explicit, frontend-safe, and operationally sustainable instead of leaving provisional `uploaded` rows undefined forever

Work:

- locked the lifecycle meanings in the backend contract:
  - `uploaded` means upload intent exists but indexing has not started
  - `processing` means indexing is currently running
  - `indexed` means retrieval-eligible
  - `failed` means indexing failed and may be retried through finalize
- implemented explicit finalize-state behavior in:
  - `apps/api/modules/documents/service.py`
  - `apps/api/modules/documents/routes.py`
- finalize is now explicitly idempotent for:
  - already indexed documents
    - returns success without reprocessing
- finalize now explicitly rejects:
  - `processing` documents with deterministic conflict behavior
  - stale uploaded rows with deterministic conflict behavior
- kept retry truth explicit:
  - failed documents remain retryable through the existing finalize path
- chose a staff-visible stale-row policy instead of background cleanup in this phase:
  - added `DOCUMENT_UPLOAD_STALE_AFTER_SECONDS`
  - default grace window: `86400` seconds
  - uploaded rows older than that threshold are treated as stale
- enriched list/detail/finalize contract metadata in:
  - `apps/api/modules/documents/schemas.py`
  - `apps/api/modules/documents/service.py`
    with:
  - `is_upload_stale`
  - `can_finalize`
  - `can_retry_finalize`
  - `requires_reupload`
  - `lifecycle_note`
- locked action truth by state:
  - fresh `uploaded`: finalize allowed
  - stale `uploaded`: finalize hidden, re-upload required, delete allowed
  - `processing`: finalize hidden
  - `failed`: retry finalize allowed
  - `indexed`: finalize returns idempotent success and no retry action is implied

Acceptance:

- the UI does not show impossible actions or misleading state meanings
- provisional `uploaded` rows do not remain forever without an explicit operational policy
- retry behavior is truthful for both failed-indexing and missing-object cases

## Security-Specific Checklist For This Module

The document module must preserve these audited truths and constraints during implementation:

- no raw SQL string interpolation for any future list/search enhancement
- no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` for document metadata, titles, filenames, department labels, or indexing error text
- no frontend hardcoded storage secrets or service-role usage
- no fake preview/download support against private-bucket object paths
- route-sensitive throttling on upload/finalize/delete/read surfaces
- structured logs for:
  - upload-url issuance
  - finalize attempts
  - finalize failures
  - delete attempts
  - read URL issuance if added
  - validation failures
  - throttle hits
- signed read URLs, if added, must be:
  - short-lived
  - scoped to one object only
  - excluded from structured logs in full form
  - treated as private, non-cacheable access artifacts rather than stable frontend URLs
- review the document ingestion / parsing dependencies used by the upload and indexing path before expanding allowed file types:
  - parser libraries must be maintained
  - unsafe or redundant parsers must not be introduced casually
  - new file-type support must not be enabled before the parser/security implications are checked against the project security rules

## Blockers And Corrections

1. Upload security hardening is not the main missing piece anymore because key protections already exist in shipped code.
2. Preview/download is now supported only through backend-issued short-lived signed URLs for the private bucket.
3. The current list contract is thin, so the frontend must deliberately use the locked cached-directory behavior instead of pretending server-driven filtering exists.
4. Failed indexing already has a practical retry path through `finalize`, so a new retry endpoint is optional rather than automatically required.
5. The module is a staff-management surface in real backend auth terms, not an admin-only surface, even though the route prefix is `/api/admin/documents`.
6. Provisional uploaded rows now follow a staff-visible stale policy with explicit lifecycle flags instead of silent indefinite limbo.

### Pass UXR1I: Frontend Input Warning Cleanup

Status:

- Implemented

Goal:

- remove active browser warnings caused by leaking non-DOM props into native inputs and by rendering immutable value fields without explicit read-only semantics

Work:

- update the shared date-range picker custom input renderer so it strips non-DOM props before rendering the native `<input>`
- keep the Flatpickr-owned input explicitly `readOnly`
- mark immutable edit-surface inputs as `readOnly` where they intentionally render a controlled value without user editing

Implementation record:

- updated `apps/web/src/components/ui/date-range-picker.tsx`
  - stripped the non-DOM `render` prop before passing props into the native input
  - kept the widget-owned text input explicitly `readOnly`
- updated `apps/web/src/components/admin-users/admin-user-edit-dialog.tsx`
  - marked the read-only email field as `readOnly` in addition to `disabled`
- updated `AGENT.md`
  - added a shared frontend input hygiene rule for future custom-input work

Acceptance:

- browser console no longer warns about invalid `render` props on native inputs
- immutable controlled inputs no longer warn about missing `onChange` handlers

# Staff Book Inventory Management Plan

## Purpose

This section records the implemented staff book-inventory
module after auditing the current books backend, PostgreSQL RPCs, auth
middleware behavior, rate-limit foundation, authenticated shell, and current
frontend module patterns.

This is now an implementation record for the staff inventory delivery.

## Current Audited Truth

The books module already exists, but it is only partially suitable for real
staff inventory management.

### Backend routes that already exist

- public catalog list:
  - `GET /api/books`

- current single-book detail route:
  - `GET /api/books/{book_id}`

- current single-book queue-status route:
  - `GET /api/books/{book_id}/queue`

- authenticated book mutations:
  - `POST /api/books`
  - `PATCH /api/books/{book_id}`
  - `DELETE /api/books/{book_id}`

### Current backend strengths

- book create, update, and delete already exist
- those mutations already flow through PostgreSQL RPCs instead of Python
  business logic
- `asyncpg` calls are parameterized; the current books service is not building
  raw SQL strings from user input
- delete protection already exists in PostgreSQL:
  - deletion is blocked when borrowing history exists
  - deletion is blocked when active waiting or notified queue entries exist
- mutation permission truth is already frozen in PostgreSQL:
  - `library._require_books_manager(...)` allows `librarian` and `admin`
- current single-book detail already includes staff-useful fields:
  - `replacement_cost`
  - `fine_per_day_rate`
  - `override_borrow_duration_days`
- current update RPC already preserves system ownership of circulation-sensitive
  status:
  - manual updates only allow `available` and `maintenance`
  - `borrowed` and `reserved` remain system-owned transitions

### Current backend gaps

- there is no dedicated staff inventory list route
- there is no dedicated staff inventory list RPC
- there is no dedicated staff detail route for inventory editing
- there are no book-specific rate-limit tiers in:
  - `apps/api/core/rate_limit.py`
  - `apps/api/core/config.py`
- the books route metadata is weaker than newer modules:
  - mutation routes do not document the full response matrix
  - books read routes do not document runtime auth truth clearly

### Critical contract correction from the audit

The earlier draft plan treated these routes as public:

- `GET /api/books/{book_id}`
- `GET /api/books/{book_id}/queue`

That is not true in the current codebase.

The authentication middleware only exempts the exact path `GET /api/books`.
That means:

- `GET /api/books/{book_id}` is authenticated at runtime
- `GET /api/books/{book_id}/queue` is authenticated at runtime

So the current situation is semantically wrong in two ways:

1. those routes look public in route shape and OpenAPI metadata
2. they are not public in actual runtime behavior

That drift must be treated as a real contract issue, not ignored.

### Current frontend truth

- the books frontend module now exists:
  - `apps/web/src/lib/api/books.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/useBooks.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/components/books/...`
- the shared staff catalog screen is now mounted under:
  - `apps/web/src/app/(admin)/admin/catalog/page.tsx`
  - `apps/web/src/app/(librarian)/librarian/catalog/page.tsx`
- authenticated shell navigation is now aligned with backend permission truth:
  - librarian `Catalog` is enabled
  - admin `Catalog` now exists
- the implementation follows the existing management-module pattern:
  - one module API file
  - one module hook file
  - cached full-directory refinement for the management table
  - shared list screen mounted under more than one protected role because
    backend permission truth allows it

## Locked Planning Decisions

1. Do not reuse the public catalog list contract as if it were already a staff
   inventory directory.
2. Mutation permission truth is no longer unknown.
   It is already `librarian/admin` in PostgreSQL.
3. Delete conflict behavior is no longer unknown.
   It is already enforced in PostgreSQL and must remain database-owned.
4. Add a dedicated staff inventory read surface first.
5. Use the existing repo pattern of staff-capable admin-prefixed reads and add:
   - `GET /api/admin/books`
6. Add a dedicated staff detail route and do not bind the staff edit flow to the
   current `GET /api/books/{book_id}` route.
7. The frontend module boundary must be:
   - `apps/web/src/lib/api/books.ts`
   - `apps/web/src/hooks/useBooks.ts`
8. The first delivery is staff inventory management:
   - list
   - detail
   - create
   - edit
   - delete
   - on-demand queue visibility for one selected book
9. Do not silently expand this module into circulation supervision or bulk queue
   management.
10. Keep the shell vocabulary aligned with the current UI language.
    The navigation term should remain `Catalog`, not introduce a second random
    term for the same surface.
11. For the staff inventory screen, use the same cached-directory pattern already
    used by other management modules:
    - fetch the full staff directory in backend batches
    - cache it in `useBooks.ts`
    - derive local search, status filter, category filter, and pagination from
      that cached dataset

## Goals

This implementation completed all of the following:

- froze the corrected runtime truth of the books module in the contract
- added a real staff inventory read contract
- added a real staff inventory detail contract
- hardened books route documentation and response mapping
- added books rate limiting to the shared backend security foundation
- created one clean frontend API file and one clean hook file for books
- delivered a shell-native staff inventory screen for the roles that actually
  have backend permission
- preserved public catalog list behavior without corrupting it to satisfy
  staff-only needs

## Security-Specific Checklist For This Module

This staff books delivery preserved these constraints during implementation:

- no raw SQL string interpolation was added for books search or filter work
- staff inventory reads and mutations remain PostgreSQL-backed and parameterized
- book-management business rules were not moved into FastAPI
- existing PostgreSQL delete conflict protections were not weakened
- unsupported bulk queue-management capability was not faked in the inventory
  list
- books rate-limit settings and tiers were added to the shared backend security
  model
- frontend copy stayed free of raw IDs where those IDs had no user-facing value
- frontend books enums and labels were centralized so the UI does not hardcode
  stale casing assumptions from older examples

## Implementation Corrections Recorded

1. The earlier draft plan treated the current detail and queue routes as public.
   That drift was corrected:
   - `GET /api/books/{book_id}` is now truly public
   - `GET /api/books/{book_id}/queue` remains authenticated
2. Mutation permission truth was verified and used directly:
   - backend access is `librarian/admin` through PostgreSQL
3. Delete conflict behavior was confirmed and preserved:
   - PostgreSQL still blocks deletion when borrowing history or active queue
     state exists
4. The missing dedicated staff inventory reads were implemented:
   - `GET /api/admin/books`
   - `GET /api/admin/books/{book_id}`
   - matching PostgreSQL RPCs
5. Books rate-limit coverage was added to the shared limiter foundation
6. The books schema layer was normalized around lower-case enum truth so the
   frontend does not depend on stale example casing
7. The frontend books management module is now implemented and mounted inside
   the authorized staff shell
8. The frontend books contract was split into:
   - one server-safe shared contract module:
     - `apps/web/src/lib/books.ts`
   - one browser-only API transport module:
     - `apps/web/src/lib/api/books.ts`
       This corrected the Next.js build failure caused by importing client-only
       session code through a server-rendered import path.

# Admin AI Assistant Implementation Record

This section records the implemented first delivery of the shared AI
assistant module. It replaces the earlier planning language with the actual
backend contract, actual schema, actual frontend surface, and actual
verification work now present in the repository.

## Current Audited Truth

The codebase now contains a real persisted assistant module on top of the
existing retrieval and grounded-answer stack.

### Implemented backend routes

- legacy retrieval debug:
  - `POST /api/ai/retrieval/search`
- legacy one-shot grounded answer:
  - `POST /api/ai/query`
- assistant conversations:
  - `GET /api/ai/conversations`
  - `POST /api/ai/conversations`
  - `GET /api/ai/conversations/{conversation_id}`
  - `GET /api/ai/conversations/{conversation_id}/messages`
  - `POST /api/ai/conversations/{conversation_id}/messages`
  - `PATCH /api/ai/conversations/{conversation_id}`
  - `DELETE /api/ai/conversations/{conversation_id}`

### Implemented backend behavior

- the real assistant surface now lives under:
  - `apps/api/modules/ai/routes.py`
- both legacy `/api/ai/...` debug routes were tightened to admin-only instead of
  remaining a weaker authenticated-only path
- the backend still uses the existing provider stack:
  - Bytez embeddings
  - Bytez chat generation
- the assistant ask contract is query-only:
  - `{"query": "..."}`
- retrieval knobs are no longer part of the frontend assistant contract
- the exact fallback answer remains unchanged:
  - `Information not found in official documents.`

### Implemented database foundation

The assistant schema now exists through:

- `supabase/migrations/20260404204919_library_admin_ai_assistant_foundation.sql`

Implemented database objects:

- `library.ai_message_role_enum`
- `library.ai_conversations`
- `library.ai_messages`
- `library.ai_message_citations`

Implemented schema rules:

- first-pass conversation deletion is hard delete only
- no dormant `is_archived` field was kept
- message ordering uses:
  - `created_at asc`
  - `id asc`
- citations persist denormalized source metadata plus `content_hash`
- `document_id` and `chunk_id` use nullable foreign keys with:
  - `on delete set null`
- RLS is enabled and forced on all assistant tables
- owner-admin policies protect conversations, messages, and citations

### Implemented frontend surface

The assistant workspace now exists through:

- `apps/web/src/lib/api/assistant.ts`
- `apps/web/src/hooks/useAssistant.ts`
- `apps/web/src/components/assistant/admin-assistant-screen.tsx`
- `apps/web/src/app/(admin)/admin/assistant/page.tsx`

The admin shell now exposes:

- `/admin/assistant`

### Implemented verification scaffold

Because the repo had no assistant database-test structure, a tracked SQL
verification script was added under:

- `supabase/tests/database/admin_ai_assistant_foundation_verification.sql`

## Locked Implemented Decisions

1. The first delivery is now available to every authenticated role.
2. The standard assistant request body contains only:
   - `query`
3. Conversation continuity comes from persisted backend state and route path,
   not frontend memory or retrieval knobs.
4. The backend owns:
   - intent resolution
   - retrieval profile selection
   - retrieval thresholds
   - document and department scoping decisions
5. The provider stack remained Bytez-based for this module.
6. The real assistant product surface is `/api/admin/ai/...`.
7. Legacy `/api/ai/...` routes were retained for compatibility and debug work,
   but they are no longer a weaker access path.
8. Conversations, messages, and citations are now persisted in PostgreSQL.
9. Citations are returned per assistant message.
10. The first-pass delete contract is hard delete only.
11. No retrieval tuning controls are exposed in the standard assistant
    UI.

## Implemented Backend Contract

The clean frontend-facing contract for this module is now:

### Create conversation with first query

- `POST /api/ai/conversations`

Request body:

```json
{
  "query": "What is the semester freeze policy?"
}
```

Implemented behavior:

- creates the conversation
- derives the initial title server-side from the first query
- stores the user message
- resolves assistant intent server-side
- retrieves grounded context
- generates the answer
- stores the assistant message and citations
- returns one normalized completed turn

### List conversations

- `GET /api/ai/conversations`

Implemented purpose:

- sidebar listing
- latest-first ordering

### Get one conversation

- `GET /api/ai/conversations/{conversation_id}`

Implemented purpose:

- load conversation metadata for the active shell state

### Get messages for one conversation

- `GET /api/ai/conversations/{conversation_id}/messages`

Implemented purpose:

- load the full ordered thread for the selected conversation

### Send next message in an existing conversation

- `POST /api/ai/conversations/{conversation_id}/messages`

Request body:

```json
{
  "query": "Can you explain who is eligible?"
}
```

Implemented behavior:

- appends the user message
- resolves intent using current query plus stored conversation context
- retrieves grounded context
- generates the answer
- stores the assistant message and citations
- returns one normalized completed turn

### Rename conversation

- `PATCH /api/ai/conversations/{conversation_id}`

Request body:

```json
{
  "title": "Semester Freeze Questions"
}
```

### Delete conversation

- `DELETE /api/ai/conversations/{conversation_id}`

Implemented first-pass decision:

- hard delete only
- no archive field
- no archive route behavior

## Database Schema Plan

These tables were created under the controlled `library` schema.

### `ai_conversations`

Implemented fields:

- `id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid()`
- `owner_user_id uuid not null references library.profiles(id) on delete cascade`
- `title text not null`
- `created_at timestamptz not null default now()`
- `updated_at timestamptz not null default now()`
- `last_message_at timestamptz not null default now()`

Implemented index:

- `(owner_user_id, last_message_at desc)`
- optional `(owner_user_id, updated_at desc)`

Implemented first-pass rule:

- no `is_archived` field was added
- the contract remains hard delete only

### `ai_messages`

Implemented fields:

- `id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid()`
- `conversation_id uuid not null references library.ai_conversations(id) on delete cascade`
- `role library.ai_message_role_enum not null`
- `content text not null`
- `intent_profile text null`
- `fallback_used boolean null`
- `retrieved_chunks_count integer null`
- `created_at timestamptz not null default now()`

Implemented enum:

- `library.ai_message_role_enum`
  - `user`
  - `assistant`

Implemented index:

- `(conversation_id, created_at asc, id asc)`

Implemented ordering rule:

- ordered thread reads must sort by:
  - `created_at asc`
  - `id asc`
- this avoids unstable ordering when two rows land at nearly the same timestamp

### `ai_message_citations`

Implemented fields:

- `id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid()`
- `message_id uuid not null references library.ai_messages(id) on delete cascade`
- `document_id uuid null references library.university_documents(id) on delete set null`
- `document_title text not null`
- `original_filename text not null`
- `chunk_id uuid null references library.document_chunks(id) on delete set null`
- `chunk_index integer not null`
- `section_label text null`
- `page_number integer null`
- `content_hash text null`
- `similarity_score double precision not null`
- `rank integer not null`
- `created_at timestamptz not null default now()`

Implemented citation-retention rule:

- store `content_hash` too
- the current retrieval RPC already returns it, and it improves citation
  provenance when documents are reprocessed later
- do not cascade-delete citations from `university_documents` or
  `document_chunks`
- persisted assistant turns are supposed to stay auditable and replayable
- if a source document or chunk is deleted later, the citation snapshot should
  still survive inside assistant history
- use denormalized citation snapshot fields as the durable history surface, while
  nullable foreign keys preserve best-effort linkage when the source rows still
  exist

## Authorization And RLS Plan

This module is admin-only in the implemented first delivery.

### Route-level protection

- implemented `/api/admin/ai/...` routes verify:
  - authenticated user exists
  - authenticated role is `admin`

Implemented route-protection pattern:

- rely on authenticated request context from middleware
- perform explicit role validation in the service or helper layer before
  assistant operations run

### Database-level protection

RLS is enabled on:

- `library.ai_conversations`
- `library.ai_messages`
- `library.ai_message_citations`

Implemented owner policy shape:

- only the owning admin can read or mutate their own conversations
- only rows connected to the owning admin's conversations can be read for
  messages and citations
- nobody else can read them by default

Implemented scope rule:

- the current assistant retrieval path already sets
  `request.jwt.claim.sub` before the document search RPC
- the new assistant persistence tables must adopt the same PostgreSQL-owned
  identity discipline instead of moving ownership logic into frontend state

## Intent-Resolution Plan

Do not let the frontend send:

- `top_k`
- `similarity_threshold`
- `document_type`
- `audience_scope`
- `department`

That surface was replaced with a backend-owned intent resolver that selects one
approved profile.

Implemented internal profiles:

- `general_university_info`
- `policy_lookup`
- `fee_lookup`
- `admission_lookup`
- `department_specific_notice`
- `document_debug`

Implemented profile mapping targets:

- top-k
- similarity threshold
- metadata filters
- whether department scoping is allowed
- whether only certain document classes should be searched

This contract boundary is now enforced. Retrieval tuning is backend policy, not
normal assistant UI input.

## AI Route Redesign Plan

### Kept for debug or compatibility

- `POST /api/ai/retrieval/search`
- `POST /api/ai/query`

### Implemented frontend-facing assistant surface

- new `/api/ai/conversations...` routes

Implemented first-pass decision:

- soft deprecation
- kept the current `/api/ai/...` routes for debug/testing compatibility
- stopped treating them as the real product assistant surface

## Normalized Turn Response Shape

For:

- `POST /api/ai/conversations`
- `POST /api/ai/conversations/{conversation_id}/messages`

The implemented ask routes return one normalized completed-turn payload
containing:

- `conversation_id`
- `conversation_title`
- `user_message`
- `assistant_message`
- `fallback_used`
- `retrieved_chunks_count`
- `citations`
- `created_at`

The frontend can append the completed turn without an immediate refetch.
