# Admin AI Assistant UI Redesign Implementation Record

This section records the completed frontend-only redesign of the Admin AI
Assistant screen after auditing the implemented assistant module, current
authenticated shell primitives, current shared sidebar system, and the
existing assistant data hook.

This is now an implementation record for the redesign pass.

## Current Module

The Admin AI Assistant module is functionally implemented and now renders as a
chat-first workspace instead of an admin dashboard page.

### Current implemented screen truth

- the route is clean and localized:
  - `apps/web/src/app/(admin)/admin/assistant/page.tsx`
  - it mounts only `AdminAssistantScreen`
- the assistant UI is now decomposed under:
  - `apps/web/src/components/assistant/admin-assistant-screen.tsx`
  - `apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-layout.tsx`
  - `apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-sidebar.tsx`
  - `apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-thread.tsx`
  - `apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-message.tsx`
  - `apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-composer.tsx`
  - `apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-conversation-item.tsx`
  - `apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-citations.tsx`
- the screen no longer uses:
  - `PageContainer`
  - the old two-card dashboard grid
  - fixed-height history and thread widget scroll areas
  - the detached bordered composer block
- the current data/state boundary already exists and is sound:
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/useAssistant.ts`
- the current backend contract is already sufficient for the redesign:
  - no backend changes are required for the UI refactor

### Current codebase strengths to preserve

- `useAssistant` already owns:
  - conversations
  - active conversation
  - message loading
  - query submit
  - rename
  - delete
  - retry and refresh behavior
- the assistant route is already isolated to one screen entry point
- the shared UI system already provides:
  - `Sheet`
  - `Dialog`
  - `DropdownMenu`
  - `ScrollArea`
  - buttons, inputs, textarea, badges
- the current shell already proves the product supports:
  - desktop sidebar collapse patterns
  - mobile drawer patterns

## Codebase Corrections To The Submitted Draft

1. The redesign is possible and should remain frontend-only.
2. The current backend contract does not block the redesign. The data model is
   already sufficient for:
   - conversation list
   - active thread
   - message stream
   - rename/delete actions
3. The current `AdminAssistantScreen` should be decomposed. One file is
   currently mixing:
   - layout
   - sidebar rows
   - message rendering
   - citations rendering
   - composer
   - rename/delete dialogs
4. The shared `ui/sidebar` provider must not be dropped into the assistant
   screen blindly. It hardcodes:
   - a global cookie key
   - a global keyboard shortcut
     This would create state collisions with the authenticated shell sidebar.
5. Mobile drawer behavior should therefore reuse the `Sheet` primitive or a
   local assistant-specific wrapper, not a second global shell sidebar provider.
6. Desktop collapse state for the assistant history panel should remain local
   UI state inside the assistant module, not shared shell state.
7. `useAssistant` should not absorb layout-only state such as:
   - mobile history open state
   - desktop history collapsed state
   - citation expansion state
     Those belong to the UI layer, not the data hook.
8. The current relative-time rendering uses `Date.now()` during render inside
   `AdminAssistantScreen`. That is acceptable for now, but the redesign pass
   should avoid increasing hydration-sensitive formatting logic in server-first
   surfaces.

## Locked Redesign Decisions

1. Keep the current backend contract unchanged.
2. Keep `AdminAssistantScreen` as the mounted page surface.
3. Do not keep `PageContainer` for the redesigned assistant workspace.
4. Do not keep the dashboard-card mental model.
5. The primary surface is the conversation thread.
6. Desktop must support a collapsible conversation history panel.
7. Mobile must use a real drawer or sheet for conversation history.
8. The composer must be visually docked to the thread area.
9. Citations must stay visible, but subordinate to the assistant answer.
10. Rename/delete actions remain in the conversation history surface.
11. `useAssistant` remains the data owner.
12. Layout-only state stays local to the redesigned screen and subcomponents.
13. Do not reuse the shared shell `SidebarProvider` inside the assistant screen
    unless it is first made assistant-safe with isolated persistence and
    shortcut behavior.

## Redesign Goals

The redesign phase must do all of the following:

- remove dashboard-style page chrome from the assistant surface
- replace the two-card grid with a real chat workspace layout
- make the conversation thread visually primary
- add desktop collapse behavior for the conversation history panel
- add mobile drawer behavior for conversation history
- redesign message rhythm and hierarchy to feel chat-native
- attach citations to assistant answers in a compact way
- dock the composer to the bottom of the main chat area
- preserve all current assistant behaviors:
  - loading
  - empty
  - pending
  - error
  - retry
  - rename
  - delete

## Final Layout Direction

The assistant should be refactored into one chat application surface with this
responsibility split:

- `AdminAssistantScreen`
  - owns assistant UI layout state
  - owns rename/delete modal state
  - composes the workspace pieces
- `AssistantWorkspaceLayout`
  - desktop two-pane shell
  - mobile conversation drawer wiring
- `AssistantSidebar`
  - new chat action
  - history list
  - refresh action
  - rename/delete row actions
- `AssistantThread`
  - thread topbar
  - message stream
  - empty/loading/error states
- `AssistantComposer`
  - sticky bottom dock
  - textarea
  - send action
  - minimal context indicator
- `AssistantMessage`
  - user/assistant bubble hierarchy
- `AssistantCitations`
  - compact attached source treatment

## Implemented File Structure

The redesign should keep the route unchanged and decompose the screen under:

- `apps/web/src/components/assistant/admin-assistant-screen.tsx`
- `apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-layout.tsx`
- `apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-sidebar.tsx`
- `apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-thread.tsx`
- `apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-message.tsx`
- `apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-composer.tsx`
- `apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-conversation-item.tsx`
- `apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-citations.tsx`

This decomposition is justified because the current single-file screen is doing
too many unrelated UI jobs at once.

## Implemented Sidebar Architecture

### Desktop

Desktop history should support:

- expanded width state
- collapsed rail-like state
- persistent toggle
- strong active conversation highlight
- compact row density
- row action menu on hover/focus

### Mobile

Mobile history should support:

- topbar trigger
- sheet or drawer presentation
- close-on-select behavior
- escape-to-close behavior through the sheet primitive

### Important implementation rule

Do not use the shared `ui/sidebar` provider directly inside the assistant
workspace unless it is first refactored to support assistant-scoped persistence
keys and shortcut isolation. The safer first redesign is:

- local `isSidebarCollapsedDesktop`
- local `isSidebarOpenMobile`
- `Sheet` for mobile history
- normal responsive layout for desktop history

## Implemented Main Chat Surface

The right side must stop reading like a bordered widget.

### Topbar

Replace the current large dashboard heading with a lean chat header containing:

- current conversation title
- subtle secondary metadata
- mobile history trigger
- optional small thread action slot if truly needed

### Thread

Use one continuous scrollable thread area with:

- no surrounding card-within-card treatment
- cleaner spacing rhythm
- natural chat reading flow
- flatter empty and error states

### Thread behavior

The redesigned thread must behave like a real chat surface, not just look like
one.

Required behavior:

- auto-scroll to the newest message when:
  - a conversation is opened
  - a new completed turn is appended
  - a pending assistant turn becomes a completed assistant turn
- preserve user-controlled scroll position during ordinary history browsing
  instead of forcing scroll jumps on every minor state change
- keep the latest turn visually anchored near the composer so the bottom dock and
  newest messages feel like one interaction surface
- avoid double-scroll layouts where both the outer workspace and inner thread
  compete for vertical scrolling

Implementation rule:

- thread-scroll behavior belongs to the assistant UI layer
- do not move this into `useAssistant`
- keep it local to the thread/layout components through refs and local effect
  logic

### Empty state

Keep the existing functional empty-state meaning, but redesign it as a centered
chat-start state rather than a dashboard card.

## Implemented Message Hierarchy

### User messages

- right aligned
- stronger filled bubble
- minimal metadata treatment
- no heavy badge header

### Assistant messages

- left aligned
- softer surface
- wider readable text column
- content-first layout
- metadata visually secondary

### Critical removal

The current badge-heavy bubble header should not survive the redesign. It is
too dashboard-like and too noisy for a chat workspace.

## Implemented Citation Treatment

Citations must remain visible, but the treatment must change.

### New citation behavior

- attach citations directly below the assistant answer
- reduce vertical footprint
- keep title primary
- keep page/section/file metadata compact
- allow future expand/collapse if citation count grows

### Rule

Citations must feel attached to the answer, not like a second dashboard panel.

## Implemented Composer Treatment

The composer should become a sticky bottom dock inside the main chat panel.

### Required behavior

- textarea with auto-growth up to a max height
- Enter to send
- Shift+Enter for newline
- pending and disabled states preserved
- send button stays visually attached to the dock

### Required visual change

Remove the current separate bordered form block and flatten it into a docked,
chat-native composer surface.

## Implemented Responsive Behavior

### Mobile

- history becomes a drawer/sheet
- thread becomes full width
- composer stays visible
- action density reduces
- metadata becomes tighter

### Tablet

- history may default collapsed
- thread stays visually primary
- header carries the history trigger

### Desktop

- history is visible by default
- history can collapse
- thread width stays comfortable and not excessively wide

## Implemented State Ownership

Keep `useAssistant` responsible for data and server interaction only.

UI-only state should live in the screen/layout layer:

- `isSidebarOpenMobile`
- `isSidebarCollapsedDesktop`
- optional citation expansion UI if added
- rename/delete modal open state

This separation is mandatory. Do not pollute the data hook with display-only
state.

## Accessibility Status

The redesign must preserve or improve:

- keyboard navigation across history rows
- focus-visible action controls
- keyboard access to row menus
- drawer close on escape
- composer label and aria correctness
- obvious disabled/pending states

# Staff Circulation Management Implementation Record

The staff circulation module is implemented. The repository now ships a real
staff circulation namespace, a database-backed supervision read model,
transaction-scoped return/renew/due-date actions, and a shared admin/librarian
circulation workspace in the frontend.

This section is now an implementation record. The passes below describe shipped
changes, not future intent.

## Final Shipped Truth

- backend staff routes now exist under `/api/admin/circulation/*`
- PostgreSQL now owns the staff read model and transaction-scoped staff return,
  renew, and due-date adjustment contracts
- selected-loan read models now carry `can_return`, `can_adjust_due_date`, and
  `can_renew` action-truth fields
- the frontend now ships `circulation.ts`, `useCirculation.ts`, a shared staff
  circulation screen, selected-loan detail, return/renew confirmations, and a
  due-date adjustment dialog
- admin and librarian shells now expose `Circulation`
- verification completed through `supabase db push`, backend compile,
  TypeScript, web production build, and targeted frontend linting

## Original Audit Baseline

Before implementation, the codebase already contained real
circulation-adjacent backend pieces, but it was not a staff circulation module
yet.

### Current backend truth

Authenticated generic borrow lifecycle routes already exist:

- `POST /api/borrow/borrow`
- `POST /api/borrow/return`
- `POST /api/borrow/renew`

Borrower-owned self-service read routes already exist:

- `GET /api/me/borrows/active`
- `GET /api/me/borrows/history`
- `GET /api/me/queue`

Queue endpoints also already exist:

- `POST /api/queue/join`
- `POST /api/queue/cancel`
- public or book-scoped queue visibility through the existing book queue-status
  surface

### Current architectural truth

The repository confirms that circulation rules are still database-owned:

- FastAPI borrow routes are thin wrappers
- FastAPI queue routes are thin wrappers
- borrow, return, and renew are executed through PostgreSQL RPCs
- self-service loan reads are executed through PostgreSQL read RPCs
- queue promotion and hold behavior remain database-driven

### Gaps Closed By This Delivery

The original audit confirmed that the repository did not yet contain:

- `apps/api/modules/circulation`
- `/api/admin/circulation/*` routes
- a staff circulation read contract
- a transaction-scoped selected-loan detail contract
- a due-date-adjustment RPC
- a circulation frontend API file
- a circulation hook
- a circulation component module
- admin circulation routes in the app shell

### Critical correction from the RPC audit

The current `library.return_book(p_user_id, p_book_id)` and
`library.renew_book(p_user_id, p_book_id)` functions are borrower-owned. They
look up the active transaction by `user_id + book_id`. That means a future staff
return or staff renew action cannot honestly be described as a simple prefixed
alias of the current self-service RPC. Staff circulation mutations need a new
circulation-facing database contract centered on the selected loan identity.

## User Flow

The correct first-pass staff flow should be:

1. Staff opens `Circulation` from the authenticated shell.
2. The screen lands on `Active Loans` by default.
3. Staff can pivot to `Overdue` and `History`.
4. Staff searches by borrower identity, book title, or exact transaction ID.
5. Staff opens one selected-loan detail surface.
6. Staff inspects borrower, book, due date, renewal count, fine state, and
   queue linkage.
7. Staff processes a return from the selected loan.
8. Staff adjusts due date from the selected loan if allowed.
9. Staff optionally renews from the selected loan only if the backend truth
   supports it without inventing staff override behavior.

The student flow remains unchanged. The database continues to own borrow limits,
queue fairness, hold expiry, queue promotion, and overdue fine generation.

## Shipped Backend Contract

The shipped first-pass route family is:

- `GET /api/admin/circulation/loans`
- `GET /api/admin/circulation/loans/{transaction_id}`
- `PATCH /api/admin/circulation/loans/{transaction_id}/due-date`
- `POST /api/admin/circulation/loans/{transaction_id}/return`
- optionally `POST /api/admin/circulation/loans/{transaction_id}/renew`

Optional follow-on reads may be added later if needed:

- `GET /api/admin/circulation/users/{user_id}/loans`
- `GET /api/admin/circulation/books/{book_id}/queue`

### Selected-loan action truth

The circulation read contract must also expose backend-owned action truth for the
selected loan.

At minimum, selected-loan detail should include:

- `can_return`
- `can_adjust_due_date`
- `can_renew`

Why this is required:

- the frontend must not guess action availability from raw loan fields alone
- return, due-date adjustment, and renew are permission-sensitive and
  state-sensitive operations
- the selected-loan surface should render only actions that the backend truth
  actually allows

First-pass rule:

- these action-capability fields are mandatory on the selected-loan detail
  contract
- they may be omitted from the list row if keeping the directory lighter is
  preferable

### Critical contract rule

Do not reuse the current borrower-owned `/api/me/*` or `/api/borrow/*` surfaces
as if they were already staff contracts. They are not.

### Staff list query model

`GET /api/admin/circulation/loans` should support:

- `status_view`:
  - `active`
  - `overdue`
  - `history`
- `search`
- `role`
- `due_from`
- `due_to`
- `limit`
- `offset`

`book_status` may be added only if the final DB read model makes it useful and
truthful.

### Stable staff loan row

The staff circulation list should converge on one stable row shape including at
least:

- `transaction_id`
- `user_id`
- `user_full_name`
- `user_email`
- `user_role`
- `roll_number`
- `employee_code`
- `book_id`
- `book_title`
- `book_author`
- `book_category`
- `book_status`
- `issue_date`
- `due_date`
- `return_date`
- `renewal_count`
- `is_overdue`
- `fine_amount`
- `fine_status`
- `waiting_count`
- `queue_status`
- `hold_expires_at`

## Delivered Outcomes

The implemented circulation module now achieves all of the following:

- establish a real staff circulation read surface instead of borrower-owned
  reads
- keep circulation rules database-owned
- expose active, overdue, and history supervision truthfully
- center actions on selected loan identity, not ad hoc book-only actions
- let staff return books and adjust due dates through approved backend contracts
- keep queue visibility read-only in the first pass
- mount a shared circulation workspace for the correct staff roles only

## Delivered Frontend Behavior

This module now follows the same management-module pattern already used in the
project:

- one module API file:
  - `apps/web/src/lib/api/circulation.ts`
- one module hook file:
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/useCirculation.ts`
- one shared staff screen component module under:
  - `apps/web/src/components/circulation/`

The first screen should:

- default to `Active Loans`
- support overdue and history pivots
- use the current shell-native management-screen language
- keep list, filters, detail, and mutations truthful
- distinguish loading, empty, filter-empty, stale, and mutation-pending states

The first pass should not invent:

- barcode workstation behavior
- hold-preparation workflows
- manual queue reordering
- manual queue resolution tools
- frontend-only state transitions

## Blockers / Corrections

1. The current repository does not have a staff circulation namespace yet.
2. The current borrow lifecycle routes are generic authenticated actions, not
   staff supervision routes.
3. The current return and renew RPCs are borrower-owned and keyed by
   `user_id + book_id`, so they cannot be treated as already-suitable
   staff-action backends.
4. Due-date adjustment does not exist yet and must be created DB-first.
5. The librarian shell already contains a disabled `Circulation` placeholder,
   but the admin shell does not yet expose a circulation item.
6. Queue visibility already exists at the book level, but queue-management
   mutations do not belong in this first circulation delivery.

## Locked Planning Decisions

1. This module is backend-first.
2. Do not treat `/api/me/borrows/*` as a staff contract.
3. Do not treat `/api/borrow/return` or `/api/borrow/renew` as already-correct
   staff wrappers.
4. Add a dedicated admin-prefixed staff circulation read surface first.
5. Center selected-loan actions on `transaction_id`.
6. Keep circulation rules in PostgreSQL.
7. Keep queue visibility read-only in the first pass.
8. Add a clean frontend module boundary with `circulation.ts` and
   `useCirculation.ts`.
9. The intended first-pass staff roles are:
   - `librarian`
   - `admin`
10. Student navigation and student route access must not expose the circulation
    workspace.
11. Do not let the `/api/admin/circulation/*` path prefix silently redefine this
    module as admin-only. Route permission must be enforced intentionally based
    on the approved staff-role truth.
12. Do not invent staff override renewal behavior unless it is explicitly
    approved and backed by the database contract.
13. Read contracts must carry enough truth for the frontend to render allowed
    actions without guessing from raw status fields alone.

# Admin Metrics Dashboard Implementation Record

The Admin Metrics Dashboard is implemented. The repository now ships a real
admin-only overview surface over the existing metrics contract, with hardened
route protection, a dedicated frontend metrics boundary, and a shell-native
dashboard screen replacing the old admin placeholder root.

This section is now an implementation record. The passes below describe shipped
work, not future intent.

## Final Shipped Truth

- backend metrics route remains:
  - `GET /api/admin/metrics/dashboard`
- runtime permission is now truly admin-only in both FastAPI and PostgreSQL
- the route now documents:
  - `401`
  - `403`
  - `422`
  - `429`
  - `500`
- the route now uses a dedicated metrics read limiter tier:
  - `metrics_read`
- the frontend now ships:
  - `apps/web/src/lib/api/metrics.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/useAdminMetrics.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/components/dashboard/admin-metrics-dashboard-screen.tsx`
- `/admin/dashboard` now mounts the real metrics dashboard instead of
  `RoleDashboardRoot`
- librarian and student dashboard roots remain unchanged in this pass
- the disabled `/admin/analytics` shell item remains untouched

## Original Audit Baseline

Before implementation, the repository already had a metrics backend surface,
but not a real admin metrics module end to end.

### Existing backend truth before this delivery

- `GET /api/admin/metrics/dashboard` already existed
- route, service, and schema files already existed under:
  - `apps/api/modules/admin_metrics/`
- PostgreSQL RPC already existed:
  - `library.get_admin_metrics(...)`
- current response already included:
  - `active_borrow_count`
  - `overdue_count`
  - `total_pending_fines`
  - `popular_books`
  - `queue_pressure`

### Critical correction from the audit

Before this delivery, the route path said admin, but runtime access did not.

Why:

- `library.get_admin_metrics(...)` still used
  `library._require_books_manager(p_user_id)`
- that database helper allowed:
  - `librarian`
  - `admin`

So the repository had an admin-prefixed metrics route whose DB truth was still
staff-visible. That mismatch is now closed.

### Existing frontend truth before this delivery

The admin dashboard page already existed, but it was still a placeholder
surface.

Current state before implementation:

- metrics fetch lived inside:
  - `apps/web/src/lib/api/admin.ts`
- there was no dedicated:
  - `apps/web/src/lib/api/metrics.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/useAdminMetrics.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/components/dashboard/admin-metrics-dashboard-screen.tsx`
- `apps/web/src/app/(admin)/admin/dashboard/page.tsx` still rendered
  `RoleDashboardRoot`

## Delivered Outcomes

The shipped dashboard now does all of the following:

- preserves the backend-owned analytics truth instead of recomputing metrics in
  FastAPI or the frontend
- tightens the backend metrics contract to real admin-only access
- exposes one dedicated frontend metrics boundary
- replaces the admin dashboard placeholder with a real overview screen
- keeps the screen read-only and overview-first
- points admins toward the operational modules that already own the real work

## Delivered Screen Structure

The implemented dashboard now follows the approved overview layout:

- KPI strip:
  - active borrows
  - overdue loans
  - total pending fines
- operational attention block:
  - overdue emphasis
  - queue hotspot summary
  - last refresh state
- ranked insight blocks:
  - queue pressure
  - popular books
- quick navigation:
  - circulation
  - fines
  - catalog
  - users
  - documents
  - assistant

It remains intentionally restrained:

- no fake trends
- no chart carnival
- no mutation workflows on the dashboard
- no color-only meaning

## Frontend Boundary

The metrics frontend is now separated cleanly from the generic admin user API
surface.

Implemented files:

- API boundary:
  - `apps/web/src/lib/api/metrics.ts`
- hook boundary:
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/useAdminMetrics.ts`
- real screen:
  - `apps/web/src/components/dashboard/admin-metrics-dashboard-screen.tsx`
- admin route mount:
  - `apps/web/src/app/(admin)/admin/dashboard/page.tsx`

The old metrics request was removed from:

- `apps/web/src/lib/api/admin.ts`

So the module now ends with one dedicated source of truth.

# Admin Metrics Dashboard Redesign Implementation Record

The Admin Metrics Dashboard redesign is implemented. The repository now ships a
Z-pattern dashboard layout, custom theme-aware chart components, and redesigned
loading and retry states while preserving the existing backend metrics contract
and admin-only runtime truth.

This section is now an implementation record. The passes below describe shipped
redesign work, not future intent.

## Final Shipped Truth

- the backend route remains:
  - `GET /api/admin/metrics/dashboard`
- the route mount remains:
  - `/admin/dashboard`
- the dashboard now follows a deliberate Z-pattern layout:
  - top-left overview hero
  - top-right operational pressure block
  - lower-left catalog activity chart block
  - lower-right queue detail and drill-down navigation
- the frontend now ships redesign helpers in:
  - `apps/web/src/components/dashboard/dashboard-visuals.tsx`
  - `apps/web/src/components/dashboard/dashboard-loading-state.tsx`
  - `apps/web/src/components/dashboard/dashboard-quick-links.tsx`
- the main screen orchestration now lives in the redesigned:
  - `apps/web/src/components/dashboard/admin-metrics-dashboard-screen.tsx`
- no backend contract changes were needed for this redesign
- no external charting dependency was added

## Current Redesign Baseline Closed By This Delivery

Before this redesign, the dashboard already had a truthful metrics contract and
working frontend boundary, but the screen still read as a symmetric card-grid
admin page:

- `PageContainer`
- top KPI strip
- second grid row for operational attention and queue pressure
- third grid row for popular books and quick navigation

That structure worked functionally, but it did not guide the eye through the
page in a top-left to top-right to bottom-right scan path.

It also lacked:

- dedicated chart primitives
- layout-matched loading skeletons
- a stronger visual distinction between overview, attention, insight, and
  navigation zones

## Delivered Redesign Outcomes

The shipped redesign now does all of the following:

- converts the dashboard into a higher-signal Z-pattern overview surface
- strengthens the top-left overview with a hero summary and dominant KPI
  treatment
- moves immediate operational pressure and queue-share context into the
  top-right
- turns popular-book insight into a dedicated chart-led lower-left surface
- keeps queue pressure detail and quick navigation in the lower-right
- redesigns loading, empty, and retry states so they match the new layout
- preserves admin-only route behavior and existing data boundaries

## Locked Decisions Preserved In The Implementation

1. The backend route did not change.
2. The dashboard route stayed:
   - `/admin/dashboard`
3. The module stayed admin-only.
4. No new analytics business logic was added in FastAPI.
5. No fake time-series trend line was introduced.
6. No part-to-whole chart was used for mixed-unit KPI values.
7. The redesign stayed shell-native and used existing theme tokens.
8. Quick links remained navigational rather than operational mutation actions.

## Theme-Aware Visualization Truth

The redesign now uses current theme tokens from:

- `apps/web/src/app/globals.css`

Implemented visual forms:

- stronger KPI typography for headline metrics
- ranked activity contour for current catalog momentum
- ranked bar charts for:
  - popular books
  - queue pressure
- a same-unit doughnut-style queue distribution for current waiting-reader
  share

Semantic rules preserved:

- mixed-unit KPI values were not merged into one composition chart
- the queue doughnut only uses same-unit waiting counts
- the ranked activity contour is explicitly rank-based, not time-based
- chart meaning does not rely on color alone because labels, values, captions,
  and copy remain present

## Responsive And State Behavior

The shipped redesign now preserves priority across breakpoints:

- desktop keeps the Z-pattern most strongly
- tablet compresses the layout without flattening visual priority
- mobile stacks the surfaces in the same importance order

The redesign also aligns non-happy states with the new composition:

- loading skeletons mirror the new dashboard shape
- quiet and empty states remain explicit
- retry/error state remains prominent without replacing good stale data

# Librarian Operational Metrics Dashboard Implementation Record

## Purpose

This phase turned the current librarian dashboard from a launcher-only landing
screen into a truthful read-only operational overview for librarian work.

This was a new phase. It was not a cosmetic dashboard tweak.

The implementation added a real librarian-safe metrics contract and then
refactored the current librarian dashboard to consume that contract without
borrowing admin-only analytics behavior.

## Current Audited Truth

### 1. The librarian shell already exposes the correct operational modules

The current librarian shell already mounts:

- `/librarian/dashboard`
- `/librarian/catalog`
- `/librarian/circulation`
- `/librarian/fines`
- `/librarian/documents`
- `/librarian/assistant`

So the missing piece is not route availability. The missing piece is operational
overview density at the dashboard itself.

### 2. The current librarian dashboard is intentionally launcher-only

The shipped librarian dashboard in
`apps/web/src/components/dashboard/librarian-dashboard-screen.tsx` is currently
honest about being lighter than the admin dashboard and explicitly says it does
not show admin-only metrics, trends, or user-management summaries.

That means this phase must change real product behavior, not just restyle
existing guidance blocks.

### 3. The current admin metrics route is truly admin-only now

The current codebase already tightened admin metrics into a real admin-only
surface:

- FastAPI route:
  - `apps/api/modules/admin_metrics/routes.py`
- service wrapper:
  - `apps/api/modules/admin_metrics/service.py`
- SQL RPC:
  - `library.get_admin_metrics(...)`
- admin-only migration lock:
  - `supabase/migrations/20260406101500_library_admin_metrics_admin_only.sql`

So the previous older assumption that admin metrics was a shared manager
surface is obsolete. It now rejects non-admin access by both route and RPC
truth.

### 4. Staff operational reads already exist, but they live under mixed route prefixes

The codebase already exposes librarian/admin-safe operational reads through
existing shared surfaces such as:

- `/api/admin/circulation/...`
- `/api/admin/fines...`
- `/api/admin/documents...`
- `/api/admin/books...`

This means the repo already contains real staff-safe operational truth, but it
does not use a perfectly clean route namespace yet. That inconsistency must be
acknowledged instead of ignored.

### 5. Queue pressure and popularity sources already exist in SQL

The database already ships materialized views for ranked operational pressure:

- `library.mv_queue_pressure`
- `library.mv_popular_books`

The current admin metrics RPC already consumes those views.

So queue hotspots can be built truthfully without inventing frontend ranking
logic.

### 6. Circulation read truth already contains overdue and queue-linked operational state

The circulation module already exposes a staff circulation read model through:

- `library.staff_circulation_directory`
- `library.get_staff_circulation_loans(...)`
- `library.get_staff_circulation_loan_detail(...)`

Those reads already include:

- active-vs-overdue scope
- fine amount
- fine status
- waiting count
- queue status

So active-loan pressure and overdue pressure are already real backend concepts.

### 7. Document attention truth already exists, but not as a standalone SQL read model

The documents module already returns lifecycle flags such as:

- `can_finalize`
- `can_retry_finalize`
- `requires_reupload`
- `lifecycle_note`

But those flags are currently derived in the backend document service layer,
not in a dedicated SQL RPC. That matters because it means a "single SQL-only
metrics implementation" would duplicate existing document lifecycle rules unless
the document read model is reworked first.

### 8. The current admin metrics headline for fines is wrong for librarians

The current admin metrics contract exposes:

- `total_pending_fines`

That is a total money amount, not a count of fine records needing review.

For a librarian operations dashboard, the correct first-pass headline metric is:

- `pending_fine_count`

not total money exposure.

### 9. The repo already has a generic metrics read limiter tier

The backend already defines and uses:

- `metrics_read`

That means a brand-new environment/config/rate-limit tier is not automatically
justified for the first librarian metrics pass.

## Corrections To The Submitted Draft

### 1. The new phase direction is correct

The draft is correct that the next phase must be a truthful librarian metrics
phase, not a frontend-only dashboard decoration pass.

### 2. Reusing admin metrics is not allowed

This part of the draft is also correct. The admin metrics route and RPC are now
explicitly admin-only and must remain so.

### 3. The route recommendation is valid, but it creates a deliberate prefix exception

The recommended route:

- `GET /api/librarian/metrics/dashboard`

is acceptable and keeps this phase semantically honest.

But it must be treated as a deliberate exception, because the existing shared
staff operational routes still live under `/api/admin/...`.

This phase must not widen into a route-namespace cleanup of the older shared
staff modules.

### 4. A dedicated new metrics limiter tier is not justified yet

The submitted draft asked for a dedicated metrics limiter tier.

That is unnecessary churn in the current repository state because:

- the backend already has `metrics_read`
- this librarian dashboard read is the same broad sensitivity class as the
  current admin dashboard read
- adding a new tier would force extra config and environment changes without a
  demonstrated need

So the first pass should reuse:

- `metrics_read`

If later operations require separate throttle budgets for admin and librarian
dashboards, that can be a later refinement.

### 5. `generated_at` inside `data` is unnecessary

The standardized backend response envelope already carries:

- `timestamp_ms`

Adding a second freshness field such as `generated_at` inside `data` would be
duplicate semantics with no real benefit in the current API style.

So this phase should keep one freshness source:

- envelope `timestamp_ms`

### 6. The backend aggregation should be hybrid, not dogmatically SQL-only

The draft preferred one dedicated SQL read function or RPC for the full
dashboard payload.

That is only partially correct.

It is a good fit for:

- active loan count
- overdue loan count
- pending fine count
- queue hotspots
- optional popular books

But it is not the cleanest fit for document attention in the current codebase,
because document lifecycle/action flags are already derived in the existing
documents service.

So the correct first-pass backend approach is:

- SQL read model for loan/fine/queue metrics
- reuse of the existing document lifecycle enrichment for document attention
- one thin route response composed server-side

This keeps the dashboard backend-owned without duplicating lifecycle rules in
two places.

### 7. Popular books should not be mandatory in the first pass

The repo already has `mv_popular_books`, so it is technically easy to add.

That is not the same thing as saying it belongs in the first librarian
dashboard.

It should remain optional and secondary because the real first-pass librarian
questions are:

- how much active work exists
- what is overdue
- how many fines still need review
- which documents need intervention
- which titles are under queue pressure

If popular books are included, they must remain a lower-priority operational
support block rather than a headline analytics section.

### 8. The dashboard should not inherit admin chart language

The current admin dashboard contains chart-heavy and attention-heavy admin
visual semantics.

This phase must not copy:

- contour visualization
- donut charts
- business-performance tone
- admin command-center framing

The librarian dashboard should stay lighter, more direct, and operational.

## Locked Planning Decisions

1. The admin metrics route and RPC stay admin-only.
2. This phase introduces a dedicated librarian metrics route.
3. The librarian metrics route is librarian-only in the first pass.
4. The librarian dashboard stays read-only in this phase.
5. The dashboard stays operational, not executive.
6. No fake trend lines, time-series visuals, or mixed-unit charts may be added.
7. No admin-only data such as user-management or role-distribution summaries may
   appear.
8. Queue hotspots must come from backend-owned ranked queue truth, not frontend
   derivation.
9. Document attention must reuse the existing backend lifecycle/action truth.
10. The response envelope keeps `timestamp_ms` as the freshness signal instead of
    adding duplicate freshness fields inside `data`.
11. The current `/api/admin/...` shared staff routes are not renamed in this phase.
12. The current librarian dashboard route wiring remains the source of truth:
    the refactor happens inside the screen implementation, not through route
    rewiring.
13. `pending_fine_count` means the count of pending fine records requiring
    librarian review, not total money exposure and not unique-borrower count.
14. `documents_requiring_action_count` must be derived from the same inclusion
    rule as `document_attention`, so the headline count and the attention list
    cannot drift semantically.
15. Ranked dashboard lists must stay server-owned for ordering and limiting.
    The frontend may render them, but it must not invent ranking, urgency, or
    priority client-side.

## Implemented Outcomes

- Give librarians a truthful operational snapshot when they land on
  `/librarian/dashboard`
- surface active-loan pressure
- surface overdue pressure
- surface pending fine-review pressure
- surface document-processing attention
- surface queue hotspots
- preserve quick drill-down into the owning operational modules
- keep the screen clearly distinct from the admin analytics dashboard

## Implemented Contract

### Route

- `GET /api/librarian/metrics/dashboard`

### Permission truth

- authenticated librarian only
- wrong role returns `403`
- unauthenticated access returns `401`

Admin already has its own dashboard contract and does not need this route in
the first pass.

### Response shape

The first-pass normalized data payload should expose:

- `active_loan_count`
- `overdue_loan_count`
- `pending_fine_count`
- `documents_requiring_action_count`
- `queue_hotspots`
  - `book_id`
  - `title`
  - `waiting_count`
- `document_attention`
  - `document_id`
  - `title`
  - `processing_status`
  - `can_finalize`
  - `can_retry_finalize`
  - `requires_reupload`
  - `lifecycle_note`

Optional and explicitly non-blocking:

- `popular_books`
  - only if kept visually secondary and operationally useful

### Contract rules

- zero values must be explicit
- empty arrays must be explicit
- `pending_fine_count` must count pending fine records, not amount totals
- `queue_hotspots` must omit zero-waiting rows
- `queue_hotspots` must be server-ordered by highest waiting pressure first,
  with stable tie-breaking
- `document_attention` must include only documents that actually require action
- `document_attention` must be server-ordered by action urgency instead of
  frontend guesswork
- `documents_requiring_action_count` must reflect the full backend action set,
  not merely the post-limit number of rows returned in the visible list
- the response must not include admin-only or finance-heavy summary fields

### Query parameters

The first pass should avoid unnecessary dashboard-tuning query parameters.

Use server-owned defaults for ranked-list lengths unless a real second consumer
appears. This keeps the contract smaller and more stable.

- end-to-end route reads against a live local Supabase stack

Result:

- the librarian dashboard is no longer just a guidance page; it is a truthful
  operational overview backed by real backend data

## Implemented File Set

- `apps/api/modules/librarian_metrics/routes.py`
- `apps/api/modules/librarian_metrics/service.py`
- `apps/api/modules/librarian_metrics/schemas.py`
- `apps/api/main.py`
- one new Supabase migration for the librarian metrics read function
- `apps/web/src/lib/api/librarian-metrics.ts`
- `apps/web/src/hooks/useLibrarianMetrics.ts`
- `apps/web/src/components/dashboard/librarian-dashboard-screen.tsx`

Unchanged in this implementation:

- `apps/web/src/app/(librarian)/librarian/dashboard/page.tsx`
- `apps/web/src/components/dashboard/role-dashboard-root.tsx`

## Final Judgment

The submitted direction was correct in principle but not fully correct in its
implementation details.

The implemented phase now consists of:

- new librarian metrics phase
- dedicated librarian-only backend route
- backend-owned metrics aggregation
- reuse of existing document lifecycle truth
- reuse of the existing `metrics_read` limiter tier
- librarian dashboard refactor without admin dashboard semantics

This keeps the phase honest. Skipping the backend contract and just adding KPI
cards would still violate the current project rules.

# Student Self-Service Master Plan

## Purpose

This section locks the student-role implementation family for the UAF Smart
E-Library & University Information Assistant.

The student phase is not one single screen. It is a set of self-service modules
built on top of the already-implemented backend contract for catalog,
borrowing, queueing, fines, results, profile management, and the assistant.

This section is a planning lock only. Each student module will be planned and
approved in detail one at a time before coding starts for that module.

## Current Audited Truth

The backend already exposes real student/self-service API surfaces for:

- public catalog:
  - `GET /api/books`
  - `GET /api/books/{book_id}`
- authenticated queue visibility for a selected book:
  - `GET /api/books/{book_id}/queue`
- borrowing actions:
  - `POST /api/borrow/borrow`
  - `POST /api/borrow/return`
  - `POST /api/borrow/renew`
- queue actions:
  - `POST /api/queue/join`
  - `POST /api/queue/cancel`
- self-service read surfaces:
  - `GET /api/me/borrows/active`
  - `GET /api/me/borrows/history`
  - `GET /api/me/fines`
  - `GET /api/me/fines/history`
  - `GET /api/me/queue`
  - `GET /api/me/result`
- profile/account self-service:
  - `PATCH /api/me/profile`
  - `DELETE /api/me`
- public result lookup:
  - `GET /api/public/result`

The student phase is therefore not blocked by backend absence.
The main missing area is frontend product completion for the student role.

The current frontend already contains student-role scaffolding, but it is not
yet aligned with the approved student module sequence:

- the protected student shell already exists:
  - `apps/web/src/app/(student)/student/layout.tsx`
- `/student` currently redirects to `/student/dashboard`
- `/student/dashboard` currently renders the student placeholder branch of:
  - `apps/web/src/components/dashboard/role-dashboard-root.tsx`
- the current student shell navigation does not expose `Catalog`
- the current student shell still exposes `/student/assistant`
- `/student/assistant` currently mounts:
  - `AdminAssistantScreen`
- no `/student/catalog` route exists yet
- the existing frontend books boundary is staff-owned:
  - `apps/web/src/lib/api/books.ts`
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/useBooks.ts`
- that staff boundary is not a valid student catalog boundary because it:
  - requires an authenticated Supabase session for all reads
  - uses staff inventory routes for list/detail reads
  - applies the staff cached-directory model instead of cursor-based public
    discovery
- selected-book queue visibility already has more than one frontend transport
  caller, so this phase must avoid deepening duplication carelessly

## Locked Phase Decisions

1. Student phase is implemented module-by-module, not as one giant coding pass.
2. Student Catalog & Book Detail comes first. The existing protected student
   shell is reused as the delivery container.
3. Student Shell & Dashboard remains deferred until after the core self-service
   modules exist.
4. Student modules must remain self-service only.
5. Student must not receive staff/admin mutation affordances.
6. Student fines remain visibility/history only in the first pass.
7. Public result lookup and authenticated student result must remain separate
   contracts.
8. Student assistant planning is conditional on assistant route/permission drift
   being resolved first.
9. No fake client-side workflow logic may replace backend truth.
10. All student modules must stay aligned with the protected `/student/...`
    shell.
11. Student catalog becomes the effective student landing surface until a real
    student dashboard exists.
12. Cursor-based catalog discovery must not be reshaped into staff-style
    full-directory inventory management.

## Student Module Order

1. Student Catalog & Book Detail Implemented
2. Student Borrow Lifecycle Implemented
3. Student Borrow History Implemented
4. Student Queue Implemented
5. Student Fines Implemented
6. Student Result Implemented
7. Student Profile & Account Implemented
8. Student Dashboard Functionalization Implemented
9. Student Assistant Implemented

# Student Dashboard Functionalization Implementation Record

Status:

- Implemented

Purpose:

- delivered a real student dashboard at `/student/dashboard` backed by one
  truthful self-service backend read contract

This phase now does the real student dashboard work that was previously
planned:

- replaces the deferred student placeholder with a real overview surface
- adds one dedicated student self-service backend dashboard endpoint
- keeps dashboard aggregation backend-owned instead of stitching many
  unrelated `/api/me/*` reads in the browser
- keeps the page read-only and overview-first

It still does not expand into:

- fake frontend-only aggregation
- admin or librarian metrics reuse
- dashboard-first CRUD behavior
- decorative analytics or trend invention
- assistant, profile, or result module rewrites

## Implemented Outcomes

## Implemented Contract

### Backend route and read model now implemented

- `GET /api/me/dashboard` now exists as a protected student self-service
  dashboard route
- the backend dashboard contract is now backed by one dedicated PostgreSQL read
  model:
  - `library.get_my_dashboard(p_user_id uuid)`
- the route remains student-owned and rejects non-student access through both:
  - request-role validation in `apps/api/modules/me/routes.py`
  - profile-role validation inside the dashboard RPC
- the dashboard contract stays read-only and summary-oriented
- admin and librarian metrics contracts remain untouched:
  - `GET /api/admin/metrics/dashboard`
  - `GET /api/librarian/metrics/dashboard`

### Dashboard payload truth now implemented

- the student dashboard payload now returns one bounded overview object with:
  - `summary`
  - `next_due_borrow`
  - `current_hold`
  - `active_borrows_preview`
  - `queue_preview`
  - `fine_preview`
  - `result_summary`
- counts and previews are backend-owned and no longer depend on browser-side
  fan-out across many self-service routes
- dashboard summary counts now reflect real student-owned data for:
  - active borrows
  - overdue borrows
  - pending fines
  - pending fine amount
  - active queue entries
  - hold-ready queue entries
- preview lists stay bounded and overview-first instead of cloning the deeper
  student modules

### Result-summary inclusion gate resolved explicitly

- the current result pipeline remains scrape-driven and separate from the new
  dashboard read model
- this phase did not duplicate LMS/result parsing logic inside the dashboard
  backend path
- the first-pass dashboard therefore keeps:
  - `result_summary = null`
    when no compact academic summary is available from a safe backend read-model
    path
- the student dashboard still links directly into the protected Result module
  instead of inventing unsafe academic-summary values

### Frontend rules now implemented

- `/student/dashboard` now exists as a real protected student dashboard route
- the student dashboard no longer mounts the old placeholder branch through
  `RoleDashboardRoot`
- the student shell dashboard item is now enabled in:
  - `apps/web/src/components/app-shell/role-layout-config.ts`
- student landing still remains:
  - `/student/catalog`
- admin and librarian dashboard routes remain unchanged
- the student dashboard now owns its own:
  - shared contract boundary
  - authenticated API boundary
  - hook boundary
  - visible overview screen
- the student dashboard stays read-only and overview-first
- the student dashboard does not expose:
  - full borrow-history screens
  - queue-management screens
  - fine-settlement actions
  - full result tables
  - fake trends or decorative analytics

# Student Catalog & Book Detail Implementation Record

Status:

- Implemented

Purpose:

- delivered the first real student-facing product surface on top of the
  existing public catalog and authenticated selected-book queue visibility
  contracts

This phase stayed frontend-first over an already-existing backend contract, but
the implementation was driven by a contract audit and frontend-boundary audit so
the student flow followed actual repository truth rather than older
assumptions.

## Implemented Outcomes

## Implemented Contract

### Backend routes

- `GET /api/books` remains the public catalog list route
- it accepts cursor-based pagination params:
  - `cursor_created_at`
  - `cursor_id`
  - `limit`
- it returns:
  - `data.items`
  - `data.next_cursor_created_at`
  - `data.next_cursor_id`
- `GET /api/books/{book_id}` remains the public detail route
- it returns one public-safe `data.book` object with:
  - `id`
  - `title`
  - `author`
  - `category`
  - `status`
  - `replacement_cost`
  - `fine_per_day_rate`
  - `override_borrow_duration_days`
  - `created_at`
- `GET /api/books/{book_id}/queue` remains authenticated
- it returns one selected-book `data.queue` object with:
  - `book_id`
  - `book_status`
  - `waiting_count`
  - `has_notified`
  - `notified_at`
  - `hold_expires_at`

### Frontend rules now implemented

- `/student/catalog` now exists as the discovery-first student route
- the protected student shell now exposes `Catalog`
- `/student` now redirects to `/student/catalog`
- student post-login landing now resolves to `/student/catalog`
- `/student/dashboard` remains mounted and deferred instead of being repurposed
- the student catalog owns its own API boundary and hook boundary
- the staff `books` API client and `useBooks` hook were not reused as the
  student module boundary
- local search and filters remain truthful to the rows currently loaded into the
  client
- selected-book queue failure remains a partial failure state and does not erase
  public detail

# Student Borrow Lifecycle Implementation Record

  borrow, renew, and return flow on top of the existing backend contract

This phase connected the implemented student catalog detail surface to the real

circulation logic out of the backend.

Validation note:

- this implementation was grounded in the current route, schema, and service
  files in the repository
- there is still no tracked local `openapi.json` artifact in this repo, so
  route/schema code remained the source of truth for both planning and
  implementation

## Implemented Outcomes

## Implemented Contract

### Backend routes

- `GET /api/me/borrows/active` remains the authenticated student active-loans
  read route
- it returns:
  - `data.items`
- each active-borrow row contains:
  - `transaction_id`
  - `book_id`
  - `title`
  - `author`
  - `category`
  - `book_status`
  - `issue_date`
  - `due_date`
  - `renewal_count`
- `POST /api/borrow/borrow` remains the student borrow mutation route
- `POST /api/borrow/return` remains the student return mutation route
- `POST /api/borrow/renew` remains the student renew mutation route
- all three current student borrow mutations use:
  - `BorrowActionRequest`
- that request body still contains:
  - `book_id`
- all three current successful mutation envelopes still return:
  - `status: 200`
  - `message`
  - `data: {}`

### Frontend rules now implemented

- `/student/borrows` now exists as the real active-borrow workspace
- the protected student shell now exposes an enabled `My Borrows` route
- `/student` still lands on `/student/catalog`
- the deferred student dashboard remains mounted and untouched
- the student borrow module now owns its own:
  - pure contract boundary
  - authenticated API boundary
  - hook boundary
- borrow action entry remains anchored to the selected-book detail panel, not
  catalog list rows
- active-borrow row actions carry `book_id` through the mutation path even
  though `transaction_id` is visible in the read contract
- successful borrow keeps catalog discovery and selected-book ownership intact
  while surfacing a real path into `/student/borrows`
- renew and return now reconcile through authoritative re-fetch instead of
  optimistic local rewrites
- the active-borrows screen stays student-facing and does not copy staff
  circulation directory patterns
- the student active-borrows screen does not invent server-backed pagination,
  search, or filtering that the backend contract does not expose

# Student Borrow History Implementation Record

Status:

- Implemented

Purpose:

- delivered the student's historical borrowing record as a separate, read-only
  self-service module

This phase now does one thing properly:

- lets the student see books they borrowed in the past

It still does not expand into:

- active borrow management
- queue management
- fines
- dashboard work
- staff circulation behavior

separation is now enforced in the UI and route structure.

## Implemented Outcomes

## Implemented Contract

### Backend route truth preserved

- `GET /api/me/borrows/history` remains the only backend route used for this
  module
- the request stays read-only
- the request uses only the supported query contract:
  - `limit`
- the current repo-validated limit behavior remains:
  - default route value: `50`
  - route validation: `ge=1`, `le=200`
  - SQL fallback for null or non-positive values: `50`
  - SQL clamp for values above `200`: `200`
- the current SQL ordering remains:
  - `return_date desc`

### Implemented row truth

- the history module renders the existing backend row shape:
  - `transaction_id`
  - `book_id`
  - `title`
  - `author`
  - `category`
  - `book_status`
  - `issue_date`
  - `due_date`
  - `return_date`
  - `renewal_count`
- `return_date` is now the explicit completion anchor in the student history UI
- `book_status` is no longer left semantically ambiguous:
  - it is shown only as current book status
  - it is not presented as historical return-state truth

### Frontend rules now implemented

- `/student/borrows/history` now exists as a protected sibling route under the
  existing borrow area
- the active borrow workspace remains at:
  - `/student/borrows`
- the student shell top-level `My Borrows` section remains unchanged and still
  covers the history route through `startsWith`
- no top-level shell rewrite was introduced for this phase
- the history module owns its own API boundary and hook boundary
- the history screen stays read-only
- the history screen does not expose renew, return, or borrow controls
- the first pass relies on the current backend history contract and does not
  invent page-number navigation or fake server-side sorting controls

# Student Queue Implementation Record

Status:

- Implemented

Purpose:

- delivered the student's real queue module as a self-service product surface

This phase now does the real queue work that was previously deferred:

- lets the student join the waiting queue from selected-book context
- lets the student see current queue participation truthfully
- lets the student cancel active queue entries when the backend allows it

It still does not expand into:

- borrow lifecycle rewrite
- fine logic
- dashboard work
- notification inbox invention
- librarian or staff queue management

## Implemented Outcomes

## Implemented Contract

### Backend routes preserved

- `GET /api/me/queue` remains the authenticated queue workspace source of truth
- `POST /api/queue/join` remains the authenticated join mutation route
- `POST /api/queue/cancel` remains the authenticated cancel mutation route
- `GET /api/books/{book_id}/queue` remains the separate selected-book queue summary route

### Mutation request truth now locked

- `POST /api/queue/join` uses:
  - `JoinQueueRequest`
  - body field:
    - `book_id`
- `POST /api/queue/cancel` uses:
  - `CancelQueueRequest`
  - body field:
    - `book_id`
- both queue mutations preserve the current normalized message-envelope shape:
  - `status`
  - `message`
  - `data`
  - `timestamp_ms`
- current success messages remain:
  - join:
    - `Joined queue`
  - cancel:
    - `Queue entry cancelled`

### Queue read contract correction implemented

- the repo had a real queue read drift before this phase:
  - `apps/api/modules/me/schemas.py` expected:
    - `position`
  - the SQL read RPC still returned:
    - `queue_position`
- this phase corrected the drift in two places:
  - `apps/api/modules/me/service.py` now normalizes legacy `queue_position -> position` before returning queue rows
  - `supabase/migrations/20260414103000_library_fix_my_queue_entries_position_contract.sql` realigns the SQL function contract to return:
    - `position`
- a later migration-hardening fix was required because PostgreSQL treats unquoted `position` as parser-sensitive in the function return signature
- the queue contract migration now uses the quoted SQL identifier:
  - `"position"`
    so `supabase db push` can apply the contract correction without changing the API-facing field name
- PostgreSQL also required the migration to drop and recreate `library.get_my_queue_entries(uuid)` because `create or replace function` cannot change the row type of an existing table-returning function
- the frontend queue API boundary still performs controlled normalization as a safety layer so the UI remains stable even if a stale environment is still serving the old SQL contract

### Queue status truth preserved

- current queue status values remain backend-owned:
  - `waiting`
  - `notified`
  - `expired`
  - `fulfilled`
  - `cancelled`
- the queue UI formats those states, but does not invent hidden queue rules
- cancel affordance is now limited to backend-supported active states only:
  - `waiting`
  - `notified`
- terminal states remain visible as read-only rows if the backend returns them

### Frontend rules now implemented

- `/student/queue` now exists as a protected student queue workspace
- the protected student shell now exposes `My Queue`
- student landing still remains:
  - `/student/catalog`
- the deferred student dashboard remains mounted and untouched
- the queue module now owns its own:
  - pure contract boundary
  - authenticated API boundary
  - hook boundary
- queue join now stays anchored to the selected-book detail panel instead of any detached queue-entry screen
- selected-book queue summary and my-queue participation remain separate data paths
- successful queue join refreshes selected-book queue summary truth
- queue workspace rows show status, position, and returned timestamps without pretending all rows are active-only or cancelable

## Implemented Passes

### Pass SQ-A1: Student Queue Contract Audit Lock Implemented

Delivered:

- audited:
  - `GET /api/me/queue`
  - `POST /api/queue/join`
  - `POST /api/queue/cancel`
  - `GET /api/books/{book_id}/queue`
- confirmed:
  - both join and cancel payloads are keyed by:
    - `book_id`
  - both queue mutations preserve the normalized message envelope
  - queue status values come from backend enum truth
  - selected-book queue summary and my-queue participation are separate models
  - the selected-book detail panel already had the correct queue-entry location
- identified and corrected the real queue read drift between:
  - `position`
  - `queue_position`
- confirmed current queue read scope remains backend-owned and may still include terminal states, so the first-pass UI cannot pretend every row is actionable

Result:

- the queue module started from real repository truth instead of optimistic assumptions about the read contract

Delivered:

  - `apps/web/src/lib/student-queue.ts`

  - my-queue fetch
  - queue join mutation
  - queue cancel mutation

# Student Fines Implementation Record

Status:

- Implemented

Purpose:

- delivered the student's fines module as a read-only self-service visibility surface

This phase now does the real student fines work that was previously deferred:

- shows the student's current fine records
- shows the student's fine history
- keeps settlement behavior out of the student shell

It still does not expand into:

- online fine payment
- waive flows
- staff fine management
- dashboard work
- backend fine-engine redesign
- frontend fine calculation logic

## Implemented Outcomes

## Implemented Contract

### Backend routes preserved

- `GET /api/me/fines` remains one authenticated student fine read route
- `GET /api/me/fines/history` remains the richer authenticated fine-history read route
- the student module does not call:
  - `POST /api/fines/{fine_id}/pay`
  - `POST /api/fines/{fine_id}/waive`

### Fine status truth preserved

- current repo truth still locks fine statuses to:
  - `pending`
  - `paid`
  - `waived`
  - `cancelled`
- the student UI renders those statuses truthfully
- the student UI does not collapse all resolved rows into one fake generic state

### Current/history overlap handled explicitly

- the repo did not actually provide a clean SQL-level split between current fines and historical fines before this phase
- `GET /api/me/fines` still reads the user's fines ledger without a pending-only filter
- `GET /api/me/fines/history` still reads the user's fines ledger with richer resolution fields, but without a resolved-only SQL filter
- this phase did not invent a fake backend split
- instead, the student fines boundary now partitions the two views explicitly and honestly:
  - current fines are derived from backend rows that still match unresolved fine truth
  - history is derived from backend rows that show resolved or historical fine truth

### Frontend rules now implemented

- `/student/fines` now exists as a protected student fines route
- the protected student shell now exposes `Fines`
- student landing still remains:
  - `/student/catalog`
- the deferred student dashboard remains mounted and untouched
- the student fines module now owns its own:
  - pure contract boundary
  - authenticated API boundary
  - hook boundary
- the visible student fines surface does not reuse the staff fine-management screen
- the student module stays read-only and exposes no settlement controls
- fine amounts and statuses are displayed directly from backend-returned values

  models

  skipped-course records are rendered from backend-returned values only

# Student Profile & Account Implementation Record

Status:

- Implemented

Purpose:

- delivered the student's self-service profile and account surface as a
  controlled protected module

This phase now does the real student account work that was previously deferred:

- shows protected identity/account details from the currently available auth
  boundary
- allows editing only the one backend-supported self-service field:
  - `full_name`
- provides a controlled destructive account-deletion flow through the real
  backend route

It still does not expand into:

- password reset inside the protected profile page

- role changes

## Implemented Outcomes

## Implemented Contract

### Backend routes preserved

- `PATCH /api/me/profile` remains the student self-service update route
- `DELETE /api/me` remains the student self-service destructive account route
- `PATCH /api/me/profile` still accepts only:
  - `full_name`
  - min length `2`
  - max length `100`
- both self-service mutations still return:
  - `SimpleMessageResponse`
  - with `EmptyData`
- the student profile module does not expose:
  - email mutation
  - password mutation
  - role mutation

### Profile truth alignment resolved explicitly

- the repo still does not expose:
  - `GET /api/me/profile`
- the repo still has a real read/write drift:
  - visible shell identity comes from `user_metadata.full_name`
  - self-service save updates `library.profiles.full_name`
- this phase did not pretend that drift disappeared on its own
- instead, the implementation now resolves it explicitly after a successful
  backend save by:
  - updating the backend through `PATCH /api/me/profile`
  - syncing the Supabase browser-session metadata display name
  - applying a client auth-provider display-name override so the shell and
    protected profile route stay visually aligned even without a profile-read
    route

### Frontend rules now implemented

- `/student/profile` now exists as a protected student profile route
- the protected shell now exposes:
  - `Profile`
    through the existing avatar-menu integration point
- student landing still remains:
  - `/student/catalog`
- the deferred student dashboard remains mounted and untouched
- the student profile module now owns its own:
  - shared contract boundary
  - authenticated API boundary
  - hook boundary
  - screen/component set
- the visible student profile surface does not reuse admin user-management
  dialogs
- the visible student profile surface exposes no unsupported identity edits

# Student Assistant Plan

Status:

- Implemented

Purpose:

- delivered the authenticated student assistant as a real protected product
  surface on top of the existing persisted assistant conversation contract

This phase now does the real student assistant work that was previously
planned:

- keeps the student assistant on the authenticated `/api/ai/conversations...`
  contract
- removes direct admin-screen mounting from `/student/assistant`
- reuses the existing shared assistant transport and hook foundation

It still does not expand into:

- legacy retrieval-debug UI

- public or anonymous assistant exposure

## Implemented Outcomes

## Implemented Contract

  route family:

# UX Copy Cleanup Planning Supersession Note

This module originally started as a dashboard-only copy/layout cleanup. That scope is now superseded.

The active implementation scope is the later section:

`UX Copy Governance And Cross-Screen Cleanup (Implemented)`

Any earlier dashboard-only section must be treated as historical validation context only. It must not limit the coding scope.

Current active scope:

1. dashboards
2. student queue
3. student borrows
4. student catalog
5. student fines
6. student profile
7. student result
8. staff result
9. staff circulation
10. librarian self-profile
11. admin user-management touchups
12. shared dashboard visuals/quick links if they still expose technical copy

Reason:

The current issue is cross-screen visible wording, not only dashboard copy. Coding must follow the cross-screen plan and the full delivery order below.

# Dashboard UX Copy/Layout Redesign (Implemented)

## Scope Decision

This phase was implemented as frontend-only.

No backend, database, RPC, endpoint, or schema changes are allowed in this pass.

## Codebase Validation Of Proposed Plan

The proposal is directionally correct and implementation-feasible, with these validated facts from the current repository:

1. The three dashboard surfaces are real, role-bound, and API-backed:
   - admin dashboard screen:
     - `apps/web/src/components/dashboard/admin-metrics-dashboard-screen.tsx`
   - student dashboard screen:
     - `apps/web/src/components/dashboard/student-dashboard-screen.tsx`
   - librarian dashboard screen:
     - `apps/web/src/components/dashboard/librarian-dashboard-screen.tsx`
2. Page routing for dashboards is already role-separated:
   - `apps/web/src/app/(admin)/admin/dashboard/page.tsx`
   - `apps/web/src/app/(student)/student/dashboard/page.tsx`
   - `apps/web/src/app/(librarian)/librarian/dashboard/page.tsx`
3. Librarian dashboard is mounted through:
   - `apps/web/src/components/dashboard/role-dashboard-root.tsx`
4. Role navigation text/config is centralized in:
   - `apps/web/src/components/app-shell/role-layout-config.ts`
5. Dashboard data contracts are already stable and separate per role:
   - admin:
     - `apps/web/src/hooks/useAdminMetrics.ts`
     - `apps/web/src/lib/api/metrics.ts`
   - librarian:
     - `apps/web/src/hooks/useLibrarianMetrics.ts`
     - `apps/web/src/lib/api/librarian-metrics.ts`
   - student:
     - `apps/web/src/hooks/useStudentDashboard.ts`
     - `apps/web/src/lib/api/student-dashboard.ts`
     - `apps/web/src/lib/student-dashboard.ts`
6. Shared dashboard UI composition points already exist:
   - `apps/web/src/components/dashboard/dashboard-quick-links.tsx`
   - `apps/web/src/components/dashboard/dashboard-visuals.tsx`
   - `apps/web/src/components/dashboard/dashboard-loading-state.tsx`

## Confirmed UX/Copy Problems In Current Frontend

These are directly present in current code and must be corrected:

1. Engineer-facing copy appears in user surfaces.
   - examples currently present:
     - "Admin Command Center"
     - "Catalog Momentum"
     - "Activity contour"
     - "Operational Attention"
     - "Pressure map"
     - "Queue entries still active under backend queue state."
     - "Academic summary is not included in this dashboard payload yet."
2. Multiple descriptions expose implementation framing ("backend", "payload", "contract") instead of user actions.
3. Section density is high in admin/librarian dashboards relative to direct user tasks.
4. Some empty-state and helper text is operationally accurate but linguistically wrong for end users.

## Locked Implementation Boundary

Do:

1. Rewrite dashboard copy to role-first, action-first language.
2. Simplify dashboard layout composition where sections are oversized/noisy.
3. Keep all current data wiring and API usage intact.
4. Keep all route paths and module boundaries intact.

Do not:

1. Modify backend FastAPI modules.
2. Modify Supabase migrations or RPC contracts.
3. Change API response shapes.
4. Change auth/role guard behavior.

## Final Role-Based Content Map

### Admin

Need to know:

1. current borrowed/overdue/fine/user/document/assistant activity level
2. where action is required immediately

Next actions:

1. circulation
2. fines
3. users
4. catalog
5. documents
6. assistant

Owning modules:

1. `/admin/circulation`
2. `/admin/fines`
3. `/admin/users`
4. `/admin/catalog`
5. `/admin/documents`
6. `/admin/assistant`

### Librarian

Need to know:

1. active loans
2. overdue workload
3. pending fines
4. document processing queue
5. high-demand books / waiting pressure

Next actions:

1. circulation
2. fines
3. documents
4. catalog

Owning modules:

1. `/librarian/circulation`
2. `/librarian/fines`
3. `/librarian/documents`
4. `/librarian/catalog`

### Student

Need to know:

1. what is borrowed
2. what is overdue
3. what is owed
4. what is waiting
5. what is ready for pickup

Next actions:

1. borrows
2. queue
3. fines
4. catalog
5. result
6. assistant

Owning modules:

1. `/student/borrows`
2. `/student/queue`
3. `/student/fines`
4. `/student/catalog`
5. `/student/result`
6. `/student/assistant`

internals) while preserving all existing API/auth/backend behavior.

     error-title wording only)

## Locked Scope For This Module

Do:

1. replace technical/internal visible wording with plain user language
2. keep copy role-specific (student/librarian/admin mental model)
3. simplify noisy/oversized empty states where needed
4. preserve existing component architecture and design-system patterns

Do not:

1. change backend code (`apps/api`)
2. change API routes/contracts/data shapes
3. change Supabase/PostgreSQL logic or migrations
4. change auth/session/guard behavior
5. invent fake data or fake chart values

## Coding Guardrails For This Module

1. Do not perform blind global string replacement.
2. Read each target file before editing it.
3. Preserve component props and exported component names unless a change is explicitly required.
4. Preserve API hooks, query keys, route links, role guards, and data mapping.
5. Do not move visible copy into API clients unless the repo already uses that pattern.
6. Do not create a generic copy framework for this pass.
7. Prefer local constants only when they reduce repeated strings inside the same file.
8. Keep UI copy truthful to available data. If a value is empty, show a clear empty state instead of inventing activity.
9. Layout simplification is allowed only where current sections are visibly oversized, repetitive, or empty-heavy.
10. Any visual/layout change must be explained as presentation-only.

## Acceptance Criteria For This Module

1. visible UI text does not expose backend/API/database implementation terms
2. role copy is distinct and aligned to user tasks
3. empty states are compact and actionable
4. all existing data wiring, routes, and auth guards remain unchanged
5. lint/build pass or exact failure details are documented
6. every file listed in the confirmed affected-screen audit is either updated or explicitly marked `no change required` with a reason
7. old dashboard-only wording does not remain in shared dashboard visuals or quick-link components
8. no visible text uses implementation terms such as backend, payload, contract, state machine, queue state, pressure map, activity contour, calculation load, or module surface
9. result screens keep academic/result wording clear without exposing parser, payload, lookup pipeline, or protected surface terminology
10. student-facing screens explain user outcomes only: borrowed books, due dates, fines, waiting list, pickup status, catalog, result, and assistant
11. staff-facing screens explain staff actions only: circulation, overdue books, fines, documents, waiting readers, users, and assistant content

Implementation status:

1. copy/layout acceptance criteria were applied across the listed files
2. API/auth/data contracts were not changed
3. build passed
4. type check passed
5. lint remains blocked by pre-existing unrelated errors outside this UX pass

# Layered AI Assistant Pipeline Refactor (Implemented)

## Scope Decision

This module was implemented in two passes:

1. backend pipeline refactor with contract compatibility
2. assistant frontend copy/alignment update

No `/api/ai` request/response schema changes were introduced.

## Pass 1: Backend Pipeline (Implemented)

### B1-B9 Files Delivered

Created:

1. `apps/api/modules/ai/constants.py`
2. `apps/api/modules/ai/models.py`
3. `apps/api/modules/ai/intent_detector.py`
4. `apps/api/modules/ai/history_selector.py`
5. `apps/api/modules/ai/retrieval_profiles.py`
6. `apps/api/modules/ai/task_planner.py`
7. `apps/api/modules/ai/executor.py`
8. `apps/api/modules/ai/response_validator.py`
9. `apps/api/modules/ai/pipeline.py`

Updated:

10. `apps/api/modules/ai/service.py` (now compatibility wrapper that delegates to pipeline)

Created tests:

11. `apps/api/tests/modules/ai/test_intent_detector.py`
12. `apps/api/tests/modules/ai/test_history_selector.py`
13. `apps/api/tests/modules/ai/test_task_planner.py`
14. `apps/api/tests/modules/ai/test_response_validator.py`
15. `apps/api/tests/modules/ai/test_pipeline_routing.py`

### Backend Behavior Implemented

1. current-turn intent classification now runs before history use
2. follow-up context is approved/rejected by explicit history selector logic
3. task planner maps routing + history into one execution task
4. executor isolates mode behavior:
   - `static_response`
   - `app_help_response`
   - `general_generation`
   - `rag_generation`
   - `clarification_response`
5. response validator enforces citation/fallback integrity by mode
6. pipeline logs structured routing metadata without full query text
7. generated-turn compatibility shape is preserved for `AssistantService` persistence:
   - `query`
   - `answer`
   - `fallback_used`
   - `retrieved_chunks_count`
   - `citations`
   - `intent_profile`
   - `applied_top_k`
   - `applied_similarity_threshold`

### Compatibility Outcome

1. existing `/api/ai/*` routes unchanged
2. existing `AssistantService` conversation/message persistence flow unchanged
3. no DB migration introduced in this pass

## Pass 2: Frontend Assistant Copy/UX Alignment (Implemented)

Updated:

1. `apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-composer.tsx`
2. `apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-thread.tsx`
3. `apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-message.tsx`
4. `apps/web/src/components/assistant/assistant-sidebar.tsx`

Implemented:

1. mixed-mode wording (general + official-document queries)
2. pending message text updated from document-only phrasing
3. citations rendered only when they exist and fallback is not active
4. conversation-empty helper copy aligned with non-document-only behavior
